# Poteto pyramid: adoption plan for TINNOX CMS

Date: 2026-11-22. Tree: `002c3d6` (`main`). Status: the open points were decided on 22 November 2026 under Michael's delegation (section 7, DEC-38), and the steps of section 6 have started. On 22 November 2026 steps 1, 2, 5, 5a, 7, 8, 9 and 10 were built on the branch `refactor/poteto-pyramid-guardrails`, one commit each with a follow-up to step 1, each reviewed on its own and the branch then reviewed as a whole; they went up as one pull request the same day and are not merged. Section 6.1 records what was built, and section 7 carries the rulings made while building. No code was changed to write the plan.

First written on 19 November against `2b5b3fc`, then brought up to date three days and 75 commits later: roles and people (stage C, migration 0013), e-mail alerts (migration 0014), and four more review records. Every section was re-checked against `002c3d6`.

The pyramid ([digest](digest.md), section 2) ranks where a rule should live, best first: (1) codebase design, (2) static analysis, (3) verification, (4) prose, (5) human review. This plan moves every rule in `AGENTS.md` and every review finding as high as it can go, and leaves `AGENTS.md` with judgment calls only.

## How to read this page

- **Layer 1** is an API or type that makes the mistake impossible to write. **Layer 2** is lint, types, a unit test over files, or a CI step. **Layer 3** is a test that needs a database or a browser, a property test, fault injection or a model. **Layer 4** is prose.
- Catalogue tests (section 3.6) need a database but read only the schema's shape, so they are counted as Layer 2.
- "Enforced today" says what would catch a violation in a **new** route, table, script or component, not only at the place that was once fixed.
- Rule ids: `AG-s.n` is the nth atomic rule of `AGENTS.md` section s (0 is the preamble). `CX`, `CU`, `FCX`, `FCU`, `SCX`, `SCU`, `LYX`, `UTX`, `SCR`, `RCX`, `RCU`, `RFX`, `EAX` and `EAU` are the review finding ids under `docs/reviews/`. `DEC` is from `docs/decisions-2026-11.md` and `P2` from `docs/portal-v2/README.md`. `OTH-n` is a rule that lives in other prose. `U-n` is a rule nobody has written down but the code depends on.
- Counts come from searches at `002c3d6`. Section 8 lists the search patterns so each count can be re-run.
- Sections 0 to 5 describe `002c3d6`, line numbers included. Where a step built on the branch on 2026-11-22 has since changed what a sentence says, the sentence carries a short note naming the step; section 6.1 has the detail.

### What was and was not verified

- Read and searched: every file named in the request, plus the code and tests the rules and findings point to. Thirteen read-only passes produced the first evidence. After `main` moved, twelve more re-checked each section against the new code and wrote the tables for the new review rounds. I spot-checked the main findings against the code myself.
- **Not run:** `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, the unit, integration and e2e suites, docker, PostgreSQL. Every "passes today" below comes from reading or from the review documents.
- Run in memory only, nothing written to the repository: the proposed import rules through the ESLint API (section 3.1), a TypeScript probe that a `pg` Pool is a valid `Tx` (section 2), `tsc` with `types: []` and other flags (section 3.5), and seeded random inputs through the domain planners and the offline-delay rule (section 4.4). The local `node_modules` predates `nodemailer`, so the typed measurements of section 3.5 were taken with that one import unresolved.
- Anything I could not settle by reading is marked **unverified** where it appears and collected in section 7.

---

## 0. Summary

**What is already deterministic.** More than the prose suggests. Forced row-level security on every table but `schema_migrations`, the `sec.*` hardening (definer, owner, pinned `search_path`, no `PUBLIC` execute), the audit timestamp and the order of applied migrations are held by tests or the database, and those tests enumerate the catalogue (`packages/db/test/rls.integration.test.ts:125-144`, `packages/db/test/sec.integration.test.ts:95-130`). The nine rows marked "done" in section 1 (AG-1.11, 1.13, 1.15, 1.18, 1.20, 2.4, 2.10, 3.5, 4.2) can leave `AGENTS.md` now; append-only `audit_event` needs the catalogue and migration-text checks of steps 5 and 6 first.

Stage C also built three things this plan would otherwise propose. The operator routes are a table, `ROUTE_PERMISSIONS`, and the API refuses to start when a registered route is missing from it (`apps/api/src/auth/permissions.ts:244-284`). A matrix test sends every route as every role and compares the refusals (`tests/integration/roles-matrix.test.ts`). And every operator write goes through one wrapper, `withOrgAllowed` (`permissions.ts:454`). The steps below build on them.

**What holds only by care.** Five things carry most of the risk:

1. **One `Tx` type for everything.** `withOrg`, `withoutOrg`, `withResolvedOrg` and `poolAsTx` all hand out the same structural `Tx` (`packages/db/src/tx.ts:10-15`, `:165`), and a bare `pg` Pool satisfies it. So an audit row on an autocommit handle, a tenant query with no organisation, and an organisation id taken from the request all compile and pass lint. The audit-atomicity bug was found three times in two review rounds on consecutive days (CX-01 on 16 November; SCU-01 and SCX-04, the same route seen by both reviewers, on 17 November). The stage C fix for another finding then added a fifth audit call on the autocommit handle (`apps/api/src/routes/auth.ts:273`).
2. **Hand-written lists.** Cross-organisation cases, audited routes, constraint names, accessibility pages and grants are lists typed by hand. One has already drifted: the table list at `tests/integration/cross-org.test.ts:730-754` omits `app`. The operator route list is now the exception, and shows the way.
3. **The e2e suite is not a check.** The job is manual (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:94-96`), nothing reads its result file, and nine of the twelve frontend findings are pinned only there. It has grown to 150 tests: about 17 minutes on a review machine, and about 20 in its one passing CI run, dispatched by hand for PR 34. The full runs recorded in rounds 6 and 8 each ended with tests that did not run. All five layouts findings (LYX-01 to LYX-05) are still open; one status card for them is proposed and waits for Michael (D18).
4. **CI cannot see an edited migration.** The runner's checksum only bites on a database that already applied the file (`packages/db/src/migrate.ts:86-89`), and CI rebuilds its database from nothing (`scripts/vitest-integration-setup.ts:11-15`). Since step 5 (on the branch, 2026-11-22), `npm run check:migrations` in the quality job compares `db/migrations` with the merge-base of `HEAD` and `origin/main` on a branch and reads the first-parent history on `main`; until a ruleset on `main` exists it reports but does not block (D12).
5. **Fixes are local, so classes come back.** The eight review records hold 103 findings. The later rounds repeat the earlier classes: RCX-01 is SCX-01's class again (a claim that outlives the authority behind it); EAX-12 found a guard that fails open, copied into six merged migrations; RCX-03 found the role matrix's own pass condition accepted a 500. Accepted residuals are recorded only in the review folders: no review finding id of any kind appears in the decisions register.

**The plan in one paragraph.** First land cheap guardrails that are green today or need a handful of fixes (sections 3 and 6, steps 1 to 12c). Then change the database API so the transaction, the organisation, the role check and the audit row are tied together by types (section 2, steps 13 to 22). Then make the error and commit-outcome handling a closed set (steps 23 to 28). Then add the missing verification (steps 29 to 37), the frontend guardrails (steps 38 to 44a) and the document checks: the control register, test tags and shortcut ledger (steps 45 to 48). Steps added by the later rounds or by a decision of section 7 carry a letter (5a, 6a, 12a, 23a, 41c and so on) so the numbers the text refers to stay stable: 59 steps in all, 48 numbered and 11 with a letter. Each check deletes the `AGENTS.md` sentence it replaces, in the same pull request, until step 47 leaves the file as the 37-line version in section 5.1.

**Corrections to the brief, found while reading.**

- The "COMMIT-outcome / SQLSTATE classifier" exists. It is `commitRefused` in `packages/db/src/tx.ts:47-54`, from finding SCX-03. There is no single classifier for statement errors: three separate readers and 24 SQLSTATE literals for constraint errors in route files, 26 with the two `55P03` at `apps/api/src/routes/auth.ts:271-272` (section 2.3; since step 7, one `23503` of these is in `apps/api/src/tags.ts`, outside `routes/`).
- The migration runner does check duplicate numbers (`packages/db/src/migrate.ts:39`). It does not check gaps.
- `AGENTS.md:36` says ownership changes use `SET LOCAL ROLE` "as in migrations 0002 to 0004". Migration 0002 does not: it re-owns its 14 functions with `ALTER FUNCTION ... OWNER TO` (`db/migrations/0002_security_functions.sql:381-394`). Migrations 0003, 0004, 0010, 0012, 0013 and 0014 do. Step 1 corrected the sentence.
- `AGENTS.md:21` names `withoutOrg` and `poolAsTx` as the only unscoped helpers. `withResolvedOrg` (`packages/db/src/tx.ts:139`) is a third way to start with no organisation. Operator writes go through `withOrgAllowed` and `withOrgDecisionAllowed` (`apps/api/src/auth/permissions.ts:454`, `:474`), which the rule does not name.
- `AGENTS.md:23` now allows one 403: a refusal by role, decided before any lookup and the same for every id (DEC-35). It does not cover the player protocol's three 403s (`docs/player-protocol-v1.md:296-297`, `:361`), one of them reachable with another organisation's display id (`apps/api/src/routes/player.ts:601-603`); it leaks nothing. And two route checks refuse by role **after** a lookup: the people routes' rank, self and grant rules, which the stage C specification allows (`docs/portal-v2/stage-c.md:104`), and the tag-scope check at `apps/api/src/routes/tags.ts:213`, `:251`, which cannot fire with today's role table. Decided: the heartbeat keeps its 403 and the rule is reworded around what a test can compare, an answer to another organisation's id equal to the answer to a never-issued id, with every 403 decided without looking up that id (D1); the people routes' rules are the one after-lookup exception and the tag-scope checks go (D20).

## 1. Rule inventory

Columns: **Today** is how a violation would be caught now. **Target** is the layer the proposal sits at; "keep" means it stays prose because it needs judgment; "done" means today's enforcement is enough and the sentence can be deleted from `AGENTS.md`.

| AG-0.4 | :9 say what was not verified; "unverified pending runtime" | Prose | Keep. It governs the content of a review report, which no check reads; what was not run is the reviewer's knowledge | keep |

**Round 5, manual user test and PR 29 review** (`docs/reviews/2026-11-19-manual-user-test/`, `docs/reviews/2026-11-19-pr29-review/`, `docs/reviews/2026-11-19-user-test-fixes-round-5/`). The manual run has no finding ids. 202 of 218 items matched, with one Major (T17.3: refused deletes are missing from the audit log), nine Minor and the rest Cosmetic. The manual test's own item ids (T1.2, T2.3, T4.2, T4.3, T4.5, T6.1, T9.3, T9.4, T12.2, T12.5, T13.1, T14, T15.2, T17.1, T17.2, T17.3) are steps of `docs/manual-test-plan.md`, not findings with an invariant; three led to product fixes and the rest to plan corrections, which the UTX rows and D3 cover. PR 29 (`86cff02`) fixed three in the product (T2.3 signed-out notice, T4.3 upload summary, T6.1 Apps filter note) and closed the rest, the Major included, by correcting the plan: a refused in-use delete changes nothing, so it writes no audit event. D3 now records that rule, and the outcome of T17.3 stands. Codex then reviewed PR 29 and raised UTX-01 to UTX-11. PR 30 (`03d74ff`) fixed UTX-01 and UTX-03 with tests that run on every push. The UTX-02 fix is pinned only by the manual e2e job. Three documentation rows are only partly fixed (UTX-08, UTX-09, UTX-10), UTX-04 and UTX-05 are accepted, and UTX-06 waits for the screen-reader pass (CMS-6, `STATUS.md:30`). No UTX id appears in a test title, in `docs/decisions-2026-11.md` or in the control matrix.

**Round 6, stage C roles and people** (`docs/reviews/2026-11-20-stage-c-roles-round-6/`, which also records round 7, the follow-up review of the fixes (RFX-01 and RFX-02), so round 7 has no folder of its own). RCX-01, RCX-02 and RCX-03 were fixed on the branch before PR #31 merged (`a7c000b`). Each fix has a test that failed in a mutation run. RCU-03 and RFX-01 are accepted residuals, and RFX-02 was fixed in wording. Record and code agree at `002c3d6`. No id appears in `docs/decisions-2026-11.md`. Control B5 and threat-model rows T3 and T17 record the RCX-01 closure. In the API, the RCX-02 rule holds only because callers run in the right order, and no test covers that order.

**Round 8, e-mail alerts** (`docs/reviews/2026-11-21-email-alerts-round-8/`). Seventeen ids: EAX-01 to EAX-14 from the static review, EAU-01 to EAU-03 from the runtime review. Commit `9a215b1` fixed ten. A test that runs on every push pins eight of them; EAX-09 lives only in the manual e2e job, and EAX-12 rests on the 0014 guard itself, with `sec.integration.test.ts` as backstop. EAU-03 (fixed later in `6a3a27b`, CMS-21) also lives only in the e2e job. EAX-14 was fixed for alert addresses, then for invites in `6a3a27b` (CMS-22). EAX-03, EAX-04, EAX-05 and EAX-10 were kept and written down in `docs/portal-v2/stage-c-alerts.md`, and EAX-13 in the `scripts/setup-dev.mjs` header and `.env.example`. EAX-11 is partly fixed. No EAX or EAU id appears in `docs/decisions-2026-11.md` or `docs/security/control-matrix.md`. B17 (`control-matrix.md:37`) describes the fixed behaviour.

| ID | Invariant | Status | What would catch a repeat in a new place | Proposal | Target |

It now measures each line's glyph box with `Range.getBoundingClientRect` and drops the `scrollHeight` test (`tests/e2e/07-apps-player.spec.ts:366-378`). Checked on macOS with Linux-like font metrics (`README.md:85`); spec 07 was not in the review's run after the fix (`:89`), but STATUS.md records it passing in the Linux end-to-end job (CMS-21, `STATUS.md:173`) | Nothing. e2e runs only when started by hand, on whatever fonts the host has. The two tests behind the failure did not run, and nothing failed because of that | Section 4.1 (the results gate fails on tests that did not run) and section 4.6 (e2e on the CI image). Step 44's stackless property tests for app rendering on Linux fonts | 2 + 3 |

Of DEC-01 to DEC-37 (there is no DEC-36), twelve state or imply a standing rule about code, plus three `P2` items. ## 2. Layer 1: API design

The database API today, quoted from `packages/db/src/tx.ts:10`, `:105`, `:126`, `:139`, `:165`, `packages/db/src/sec.ts:477`, `:494`, `apps/api/src/db-helpers.ts:32` and `apps/api/src/auth/permissions.ts:454`, `:474`:

```ts
export interface Tx { query<R>(text: string, values?: readonly unknown[]): Promise<QueryResult<R>> }
export function withOrg<T>(pool: Pool, orgId: string, fn: (tx: Tx) => Promise<T>): Promise<T>
export function withoutOrg<T>(pool: Pool, fn: (tx: Tx) => Promise<T>): Promise<T>
export function withResolvedOrg<T>(pool: Pool, fn: (tx: Tx, enterOrg: (orgId: string) => Promise<void>) => Promise<T>): Promise<T>
export function poolAsTx(pool: Pool): Tx
export function auditScoped(tx: Tx, event: AuditInput): Promise<void>
export function auditUnscoped(tx: Tx, event: AuditInput): Promise<void>
export function withOrgDecision<T>(pool: Pool, orgId: string, fn: (tx: Tx) => Promise<Decision<T>>): Promise<T>
export function withOrgAllowed<T>(ctx: AppContext, request: FastifyRequest, fn: (tx: Tx, role: Role) => Promise<T>, options?: { required?: Requirement; lockMembership?: boolean }): Promise<T>
export function withOrgDecisionAllowed<T>(ctx: AppContext, request: FastifyRequest, fn: (tx: Tx, role: Role) => Promise<Decision<T>>, options?: { required?: Requirement; lockMembership?: boolean }): Promise<T>
```

`Tx` is structural with one method. A type probe compiled, with no diagnostics, `const t: Tx = pool`, `auditScoped(pool, ...)` and a hand-made `{ query }` inside `apps/api/src`. The pool is public: `packages/db/src/index.ts:1` re-exports it, `AppContext.pool` carries it (`apps/api/src/context.ts:12`), and 35 places in `apps/api/src` read it. Operator writes now go through `withOrgAllowed`, which takes the organisation from the session and re-reads the member's role as its first statement (`permissions.ts:454-471`); `withOrgDecision` has no caller left (`withOrgDecisionAllowed` calls `withOrgAllowed`, `permissions.ts:480`).

What a new handler can get wrong today while compiling and passing lint:

| Mistake | What stops it today |
|---|---|
| (i) a change with no audit row | hand-written route tests only |
| (ii) the audit row on another transaction | RLS refuses `auditScoped` with no organisation (inferred from `db/migrations/0001_schema.sql:439`, **not run**); nothing for a second `withOrg`, or for `auditUnscoped` on the pool |
| (iii) a tenant query on the pool or in `withoutOrg` | RLS: zero rows, writes refused. Silent for reads |
| (iv) a nested `withOrg` | nothing |
| (v) a `tx` used after its callback | nothing (`no-floating-promises` catches only the un-awaited form) |
| (vi) an organisation id from the request | a UUID regex (`tx.ts:110`) |
| (vii) a write in plain `withOrg`, skipping the role re-check | a per-file count of plain `withOrg` calls in the operator route files (`apps/api/src/auth/write-guard.test.ts:21-58`); nothing outside `routes/` |

### 2.1 Audit writes that can happen outside the change's transaction

**Census** (`apps/api/src`, tests excluded): 50 `auditScoped` and 8 `auditUnscoped` calls.

- 41 `auditScoped` calls are lexically inside the callback and take its `tx`. Six are in helpers that receive the caller's `tx` (`apps/api/src/publishing.ts:845`, `:891`; `routes/schedules.ts:199`; `routes/layouts.ts:174`; `routes/members.ts:231`; `alerts.ts:201`). Three are audit-only transactions (`auth/session.ts:91`, `routes/media.ts:167`, `auth/permission-audit.ts:78`); the last writes `security.permission_denied` after the refused write has rolled back, by design (`docs/security/control-matrix.md:36`).
- Three `auditUnscoped` calls use the callback's `tx` (`routes/player.ts:215`, `:278`; `routes/auth.ts:229`).
- **Five `auditUnscoped` calls are on the autocommit handle**: `routes/player.ts:136` and `routes/auth.ts:148`, `:174`, `:191`, `:273`. `:174` and `:191` follow `loginStateTake` at `auth.ts:139-145`, which consumed the one-time login state in an earlier autocommit statement. `:273` records a sign-in whose transaction rolled back on a lock timeout, added for SCR-07 (`docs/reviews/2026-11-20-stage-c-review/README.md:35`). No credential changes on these paths. All five are accepted as standalone refusal events, written through `auditStandalone` (D2).
- `routes/auth.ts:389` revokes a session in a `catch` with no audit row: the accepted CX-01 residual, written as an ordinary call.
- Three places swallow an audit failure: `routes/player.ts:174`, `auth/session.ts:101`, `auth/permission-audit.ts:96-98`.

**Step A. A transaction is a different type from a pool.** Small, and it removes the SCU-01 shape outright.

```ts
// packages/db/src/tx.ts
declare const inTransaction: unique symbol;                       // not exported
export interface Autocommit { query<R>(text: string, values?: readonly unknown[]): Promise<QueryResult<R>> }
export interface Tx extends Autocommit { readonly [inTransaction]: true }   // only run() makes one
export function poolAsTx(pool: Pool): Autocommit;

// packages/db/src/sec.ts
export function auditScoped(tx: Tx, e: AuditInput): Promise<void>;
export function auditUnscoped(tx: Tx, e: AuditInput): Promise<void>;
type NoObject = { actorId: null; objectType: null; objectId: null; detail: Record<string, unknown> | null };
export type StandaloneAuditEvent =                                                    // refusals only: no success variant (D2)
  | (NoObject & { action: 'auth.login'; actorType: 'anonymous'; result: 'denied' | 'failure' })
  | (NoObject & { action: 'player.auth_rejected'; actorType: 'player'; result: 'denied' });
export function auditStandalone(pool: Pool, e: StandaloneAuditEvent): Promise<void>;   // a refusal with no change to pair with
export function sessionRevokeUnaudited(pool: Pool, idHash: Buffer): Promise<boolean>; // the one accepted exception, by name
```

Mutating `sec.ts` wrappers (`sessionCreate`, `sessionRevoke`, `sessionRevokeMembership`, `pairingCreate`, `pairingPoll`, `pairingComplete`, `pairingClaim`, `pairingCancelClaims`) take `Tx`, as do `lockOrgMembership` and `membershipForCheck` (`packages/db/src/members.ts:15`, `:42`), whose transaction lock and `FOR SHARE` mean nothing on an autocommit handle. Lookups and purges take `Autocommit`. `loginStatePut` and `loginStateTake` also write (the take consumes the one-time state) and are called on the autocommit handle at `routes/auth.ts:102`, `:140`, `:145`, `:303`; they take `Autocommit`, and the take, the provider call and the denial do not become one transaction (D2). The scoped refusal-only transactions of SCR-32 (permission denied, CSRF rejected, upload refusals) are the second named exception. Compile errors on day one, fixed in the same pull request: the five audit calls above move to `auditStandalone`, and `auth.ts:389` moves to `sessionRevokeUnaudited`, which makes the residual searchable. A cast can still forge a `Tx`, so add a lint ban on `as Tx` outside `packages/db`.

It cannot prevent: a change in one `withOrg` and its audit in a second one; a change with no audit at all. The type stops a success event on the autocommit handle only; steps 16 and 19 close the rest.

**Step B. The wrapper writes the audit row.** This is the step that makes "audit on another transaction" unrepresentable, because no audit function takes a `tx` any more.

```ts
export type Audit = AuditEvent | readonly AuditEvent[] | Unaudited;
export function unaudited(why: 'nothing_changed' | 'idempotent_replay' | 'telemetry'): Unaudited;

changeOrg<T>(org: OrgId, permit: WritePermit, fn: (tx: OrgTx) => Promise<{ value: T; audit: Audit }>): Promise<T>;
changePreOrg<T>(fn: (tx: PreOrgTx) => Promise<{ value: T; audit: Audit }>): Promise<T>;
readOrg<T>(org: OrgId, fn: (tx: OrgReadTx) => Promise<T>): Promise<T>;          // BEGIN READ ONLY
```

`WritePermit` is created only by the role guard (section 2.5, SCR-34); routes with no role, such as the player's, use `changePreOrg` or a permit-free `changeDeviceOrg` for the device credential.

The wrapper inserts the audit rows it is handed, then sends `COMMIT`. A change without an audit row needs an explicit `unaudited(reason)`, which is a token a reviewer can search for. "Audit, then throw" (rule U-4) can no longer be written: a refusal that must leave a trace returns `{ value: refuse(error), audit }`, which is what `withOrgDecisionAllowed` does by convention today.

Cost: 49 writing call sites (32 `withOrgAllowed`, 3 `withOrgDecisionAllowed`, 8 plain `withOrg`, 2 `withoutOrg`, 4 `withResolvedOrg`) and 34 reading ones; six helpers return the event and stop writing it. The 35 operator writes already pass through one wrapper, `withOrgAllowed` (`apps/api/src/auth/permissions.ts:454-471`), which can become the first user of `changeOrg`. The read/write split is a classification by reading, to be re-checked before `readOrg` is made `READ ONLY` (**unverified**: whether every read handler works in a read-only transaction).

**Step C. A backstop in the integration suite (Layer 3).** Types cannot see a false `unaudited(...)`. Install, from the integration global setup, row triggers that mark a transaction as owing an audit row when it changes an audited table, and fail the commit when none was written. `tests/integration/credential-audit.test.ts:44-53` already installs a test-only trigger this way. Every route any test touches is then checked for free. This needs a spike first: trigger cost on bulk inserts, the exempt tables (`display_delivery`, and the list D3 names: the heartbeat log, login state, the purges, session `last_seen_at`, the pairing wrong-attempt counter), and the right SQLSTATE. Promoting it to a numbered migration is a later decision.

**Completeness, alongside.** Type `AuditInput.action` as the `AuditAction` union and make the event a discriminated union (AG-3.3). `packages/db` takes it as a type-only dependency: `import type { AuditAction }` from `@tinnox/contracts`, never a value, and the union stays in `packages/contracts/src/audit.ts`, its one source (D19, step 17). Then two table-driven tests: every non-GET route has an entry in an expectations map (actions, or `none` with a closed reason, D3), and every audit action has an audit-failure case. For the operator routes the map exists already: `ROUTE_PERMISSIONS` (`apps/api/src/auth/permissions.ts:62-145`) is keyed by route and checked against the registered routes at start-up (`:244-284`), so the audit expectation can be a field there, leaving the sign-in, alert-confirmation and player routes. Today 5 of the 46 actions written in `apps/api/src` have an audit-failure case.

| Option | Prevents | Cannot prevent | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| A. `Tx` versus `Autocommit` | a success audit or a mutating `sec.*` call on a pool handle; refusals with no change to pair with go through `auditStandalone` (D2) | two transactions in sequence; a missing audit | 6 call sites in `apps/api/src`, plus about 49 in tests, which need a test helper that opens a real transaction (the `@tinnox/db/testing` entry of section 2.4) |
| B. wrapper writes the returned audit | (ii) entirely; audit-then-throw; (i) without an explicit `unaudited` | a false `unaudited`; effects outside the database (the alert e-mails, `apps/api/src/alerts.ts:120`, `routes/alert-recipients.ts:131`) | 83 call sites, 6 helpers; one route file per pull request |
| C. audit-debt guard | (i) for audited tables, whatever the TypeScript says | exempt tables; wrong audit content | a spike, then about 15 triggers |
| `tx.audit(event)` method | (ii) | (i); audit-then-throw | 58 calls. Subsumed by B; not recommended |

**Recommendation:** A, then C as a test-time guard, then B behind a lint with a shrinking allow-list (steps 14, 16, 19 and 20 in section 6; the completeness tests are step 17).

### 2.2 Cross-organisation responses must be 404

**How the status is decided today.** `HttpError(statusCode: number, code: ApiErrorCode, ...)` accepts any number with any code (`apps/api/src/errors.ts:9-28`). `notFound()` (`:30-31`) and, since stage C, `forbidden()` (`apps/api/src/auth/permissions.ts:288-289`) are the only helpers. No route calls `reply.code(4xx)`: all 72 refusals are `new HttpError(`, or one of two subclasses that pass a fixed status to `super` (`PermissionDeniedError`, `auth/permissions.ts:292-299`; `RefusedConflict`, `routes/members.ts:116-127`), and each code happens to have one status. The handler maps SQLSTATE `42501` to 404 (`:158-163`) and any other SQLSTATE to 500, except that writes through `withOrgAllowed` turn `55P03` into 503 first (`auth/permissions.ts:424-445`).

Row-level security makes another organisation's row invisible, so no handler can tell "foreign" from "unknown". I found no status, message or constraint error that leaks existence. What is not enforced is the shape.

Stage C added a second rule to the same line: "A refusal by role is decided before any lookup and is the same 403 for every id (DEC-35)" (`AGENTS.md:23`). The role guard is a `preHandler` after the session and CSRF checks (`apps/api/src/app.ts:117-119`), so it runs before the handler parses the id. `docs/portal-v2/stage-c.md:98-107` lists every answer, including one 403 that does depend on a lookup: the people routes' rank, self and grant rules, "after the target is read" (`:104`). The rule is reworded around what a test can compare: another organisation's id gets exactly the answer a never-issued id gets, and every 403 is decided without looking up the requested id (D1); the people routes' rules are the one after-lookup exception (D20).

| 403 site | Reachable with another organisation's id? |
|---|---|
| `apps/api/src/auth/session.ts:120`, `:129`, `:138` (origin, CSRF) | No. The hook runs before the route and reads no id |
| `apps/api/src/auth/permissions.ts:378` `forbidden` (role guard) | Yes, before any lookup: decided from the session's role alone, the same answer for every id. Tested below |
| `auth/permissions.ts:401` `PermissionDeniedError`, reason `role_changed` (`assertStillAllowed`, the first statement of every write) | Yes, before any lookup of the id: it reads only the caller's own member row. `apps/api/test/roles.integration.test.ts:311` |
| `apps/api/src/routes/members.ts:205`, `:207` (self, rank; `lockTarget`), `:308` (grant, after `lockTarget`), `:574` (rank, invite cancel) | No: the target is read under row-level security first, so another organisation's id is 404, with the code and message of an unknown id (`tests/integration/cross-org.test.ts:324-371`). Inside the organisation the 403 depends on the lookup, as `stage-c.md:104` specifies |
| `routes/members.ts:490` (grant, new invite) | No id in the path; decided before any lookup |
| `routes/tags.ts:213`, `:251` (`requirePermission` on the stored tag's scope) | No: another organisation's tag is 404 first. A refusal by role after a lookup, which `AGENTS.md:23` forbids; unreachable today, because every role that holds `content.write` or `content.delete` also holds `screen.setup` (`packages/domain/src/permissions.ts:12-26`). Both go in step 28, and PATCH and DELETE become all-of rules the guard decides (D20) |
| `routes/audit.ts:42` `forbidden` (unknown role) | No id; unreachable, since the guard refuses an unknown role first (`auth/permissions.ts:350-352`) |
| `apps/api/src/routes/player.ts:503` `url_signature_invalid` | Yes, before any lookup; the same answer as for an unknown id |
| `routes/player.ts:521` `url_expired` | Only with a validly signed URL |
| `routes/player.ts:602` `forbidden` | Yes: the heartbeat's `displayId` differs from the credential's. A string comparison with no lookup, so nothing leaks. `tests/integration/cross-org.test.ts:691` asserts only `>= 400`. It stays: `docs/player-protocol-v1.md` section 10 tells a player what to do on it, and a 404 would merge two states (D1) |

Not-found is written four ways by hand (`rowCount !== 1`, `rows[0] === undefined`, `dto === null`, a count comparison) across 53 `notFound(` calls. References inside a request body answer 404 in six places and 400 with field paths in three (playlist items, schedules, layouts).

**Proposal.**

1. **Conformance test first** (it pins today's behaviour before anything moves). Main has built part of it. `tests/integration/routes.ts:14-77` lists the 62 operator routes, and `tests/integration/session-csrf.test.ts:42-62` fails when the application serves an `/api` path from `ROUTES` that the list lacks, or the reverse; `createRouteCheck` (`apps/api/src/auth/permissions.ts:266-284`, hooked at `apps/api/src/app.ts:117`) refuses to start when an operator route has no permission entry; `tests/integration/cross-org.test.ts:324-371` compares code and message for another organisation's id and an unknown id on six member, invite and alert-address writes. Still missing: in `tests/integration/cross-org.test.ts`, for every route Fastify registered with a path parameter (36 of 76) and every body reference, the answer to another organisation's id deep-equals the answer to an id that was never issued, except `requestId`. Routes come from Fastify's `onRoute` hook, so `DELETE /registration` (a literal path at `routes/player.ts:374`) and the other player routes, which neither list covers, are included. A registered route with no row in the table fails: "No cross-organisation case for PATCH /api/x/:id". The heartbeat is pinned at 403 `forbidden` for a foreign display, an unknown id and a second display of the player's own organisation, which the fixture must seed (D1). Of the 14 range-status sites (18 assertion lines; a `>= 400` / `< 500` pair counts once), four of the sites are in `tests/integration/cross-org.test.ts` (`:543-544`, `:684`, `:691`, `:702`); this replaces them.
2. **Status from the code.** A `STATUS_OF` table in `packages/contracts/src/errors.ts` typed `satisfies Record<ApiErrorCode, number>`; `new HttpError(code, message)` derives the status. Seventy-two mechanical edits, and the two subclasses drop their status. `new HttpError(403, 'not_found', ...)` can then not be written.
3. **One lookup helper.** `found(row, 'playlist')` returns the row or throws 404. It becomes the only way to turn "no row" into an answer; the 53 sites move over time.
4. **403 leaves the route files.** The role refusals already live in `apps/api/src/auth/permissions.ts`: `forbidden()`, thrown by the guard before the handler runs (`:378`), and `PermissionDeniedError`, thrown by `assertStillAllowed` (`:401`) after reading only the caller's own member row. Named constructors for the CSRF and player-protocol refusals join them in `apps/api/src/auth/`, and the people-route rules get their own (today `refused()`, `routes/members.ts:100-111`), so the one 403 allowed after a lookup has a name. Then lint, for `apps/api/src/routes/**` and `publishing.ts`, a `new HttpError(` whose code is one whose `STATUS_OF` is 403. The list of codes is derived from `STATUS_OF` in the config, not typed, because once item 2 lands no route passes a numeric status and a selector on the literal 403 would stop matching:

   ```js
   { selector: `NewExpression[callee.name='HttpError'] > Literal:first-child[value=/^(${CODES_WITH_STATUS_403.join('|')})$/]`,
     message: 'A tenant route never answers 403. A row of another organisation is invisible: answer with found() or notFound(). CSRF, signed-URL and role refusals live in apps/api/src/auth/.' }
   ```

   plus `no-restricted-imports` of `forbidden`, `requirePermission` and `PermissionDeniedError` in the same files, so a route cannot refuse by role after its own lookup. Allowed, each with its reason in the config: the people-route constructor in `routes/members.ts` (`stage-c.md:104`) and `routes/audit.ts:42` (unreachable). The guard's 403 needs no exception: it is thrown in `auth/`, before the handler. The lint alone cannot stop a route calling an `auth/` constructor after its own lookup; item 1's test holds that (D1).

   It flags three sites today (`player.ts:503`, `:521`, `:602`), which move in the same pull request, and two imports: `routes/members.ts:40` (`PermissionDeniedError`, which moves behind the people-route constructor) and `routes/tags.ts:22` (`requirePermission`, used at `:213` and `:251`). The tag-scope checks go in the same pull request (D20), with the PATCH handler's scope pre-read that only served them (`routes/tags.ts:206-212`; the `UPDATE`'s row count already answers 404, `:219`) and `tagScopeFromRow` (`auth/permissions.ts:37`, `:216`). PATCH and DELETE `/api/tags/:id` become all-of in `ROUTE_PERMISSIONS` (`auth/permissions.ts:109-110`): `content.write` with `screen.setup`, and `content.delete` with `screen.setup`. The guard then decides before any lookup, a later split of the keys refuses instead of widening, and today's four roles get the same answers. The stage C specification (`docs/portal-v2/stage-c.md:37`, `:44`, `:956-957`, `:990`, `:1010`, `:1641`), the portal's mirror of the rule (`apps/portal/src/features/settings/TagsPage.tsx:82-83`) and `apps/api/src/auth/permissions.test.ts:124-125`, `:239` change with it. POST keeps its per-scope rule (`tagScopeFromBody`, `auth/permissions.ts:108`); that asymmetry is recorded.

"The same 403 for every id" is already tested: `tests/integration/roles-matrix.test.ts:143-153` sends every refused route with an `:id`, for every role, an unknown id and the caller's own display and playlist ids, and compares status, code, message and details (`:122-141` checks every route and role against an oracle written in the test); `tests/integration/cross-org.test.ts:301-322` adds another organisation's id for one route (a Viewer's `PATCH /api/displays/:id`). Adding a foreign id to the roles-matrix loop would cover every route.

It cannot see timing or lock oracles (migration 0012 fixed one of those); those stay with review and the model test in section 4.7.

### 2.3 The COMMIT-outcome and SQLSTATE classifier

**COMMIT outcome, as built (finding SCX-03).** `commitRefused(error)` at `packages/db/src/tx.ts:47-54` is called once, in `run()` at `:74-78`, so `withOrg`, `withoutOrg` and `withResolvedOrg` all get it. A `pg.DatabaseError` at `COMMIT` with SQLSTATE class 23 or 40, except `40003`, means the server rolled back and is rethrown unchanged. Anything else (a lost connection, a timeout, `40003`, a non-pg error) becomes `CommitOutcomeUnknownError` (`:30-35`). Tests: `packages/db/src/tx.test.ts:116-172` with a fake pool; `apps/api/test/upload.integration.test.ts:681` and `:733` through `losingCommitAnswers` (`apps/api/test/support.ts:758-789`).

Gaps:

- The caller's duty is a doc comment (`tx.ts:24-29`). `withOrg` returns `Promise<T>`; nothing in the type mentions a third outcome. Three callers check it (`apps/api/src/routes/media.ts:343`, `apps/api/src/media/pipeline.ts:674`, and `scripts/provision-member.ts:82`, which tells staff to look before running the command again).
- The HTTP error handler has no branch for it: the client gets a generic 500, "Something went wrong. Try again." (`apps/api/src/errors.ts:179-180`).
- Catch-and-re-run already exists at `routes/playlists.ts:620-637`, `routes/push.ts:189-207` and `routes/auth.ts:386-403`. All three are idempotent today; nothing requires the next copy to be. Stage C adds three catch-and-record sites that write a refusal or failure in a second transaction after the first rolled back (`apps/api/src/auth/permissions.ts:431-433`, `routes/members.ts:167-174`, `routes/auth.ts:271-283`); each is right only because its error is raised before `COMMIT`.
- E-mail is new work outside the database, done after a commit rather than undone after a rollback. The alert check claims a transition and its audit row in one `withOrg`, then mails (`apps/api/src/alerts.ts:95-120`); `POST /api/alert-recipients` stores the address, then sends the confirmation (`routes/alert-recipients.ts:86-131`). Neither tells an unknown outcome from a rollback: after `CommitOutcomeUnknownError` the claim may have committed and no e-mail goes out (counted as `failed`, `alerts.ts:96-102`), and the address may be stored with no confirmation sent while the operator sees a generic 500. Both stay at most once, as `alerts.ts:6-8` intends, but through a catch-all, not a branch a reviewer can see. No test loses a `COMMIT` answer on these paths.
- No integration test produces a real refusal at `COMMIT`, although the schema has a deferred constraint to do it with (`db/migrations/0001_schema.sql:260`). The "refused" arm is tested only with a hand-built error.
- The migration runner does not use the classifier: any `COMMIT` error is reported as a failed migration (`packages/db/src/migrate.ts:104-108`).
- Autocommit statements through `poolAsTx` bypass `run()` altogether.

**Statement errors, as built.** There is no single classifier. Three readers: `pgErrorCode` (`apps/api/src/errors.ts:53-57`; the handler, the write wrapper's lock-timeout mapping at `apps/api/src/auth/permissions.ts:435`, and 18 route sites in eleven files), `violatedConstraint` (`packages/db/src/constraints.ts:72-76`; eight route sites and `scripts/lib/provision.ts:433`) and `commitRefused`. Route files hold 26 SQLSTATE string literals, four of them from stage C (`routes/auth.ts:271`, `:272`, both `55P03`; `routes/members.ts:149`, `:164`); `auth/permissions.ts:411` holds one more outside the route files. Nine sites map a bare `23503` to 409 with no constraint name (`routes/playlists.ts:354`, `:628`; `push.ts:196`; `schedules.ts:217`, `:337`; `layouts.ts:192`, `:298`; `apps.ts:214`; `media.ts:666`), so each also swallows any foreign key added later. An unmapped `23503` is a 500, while `docs/dev-handoff.md:145` says 404. `constraints.ts` is a hand-kept list of 19 names (six from 0013 in `STAGE_C_CONSTRAINTS`, `:56-69`); each is proven by a targeted violation, but no test compares the list with the catalogue. Already missing: `playlist_item_app_fk`, `schedule_default_playlist_fk`, `schedule_event_playlist_fk`, `layout_zone_playlist_fk`, `publication_idempotency`, three `*_playlist_guard` trigger names, and the unnamed foreign keys of 0001; from 0013 and 0014, `membership_removed_by_fk`, `member_invite_invited_by_fk`, `member_invite_closed_by_fk`, `member_invite_fulfilled_fk`, `display_alert_display_fk`, `alert_recipient_created_by_fk`, `alert_recipient_token_key`, the unnamed `UNIQUE (org_id, email)` of `alert_recipient` (`db/migrations/0014_display_alerts.sql:72`) and the unnamed organisation foreign keys of `member_invite` and `alert_recipient`. `scripts/lib/provision.ts:422` types one more name as its own literal, `membership_oidc_sub_key`.

**Proposal.**

```ts
// packages/db/src/outcome.ts: the only file that reads .code or .constraint
export type DbFailure =
  | { kind: 'constraint'; name: ConstraintName }
  | { kind: 'unmapped_constraint'; sqlstate: string; name: string | null }
  | { kind: 'denied' }                                             // 42501
  | { kind: 'transient'; sqlstate: '40001' | '40P01' | '55P03' | '57014' }
  | { kind: 'other'; sqlstate: string | null };
export function classifyDbError(error: unknown): DbFailure | null;

export type TxEnd<T> =
  | { end: 'committed'; value: T }
  | { end: 'rolled_back'; error: unknown; failure: DbFailure | null }
  | { end: 'unknown'; cause: unknown };
export function settle<T>(work: Promise<T>): Promise<TxEnd<T>>;    // wraps any of the three openers
```

A caller with effects outside the database switches on `end` with a `never`-typed default, so the third outcome cannot be forgotten and a new one cannot be added silently:

```ts
const r = await settle(withOrg(pool, orgId, fn));
switch (r.end) {
  case 'committed':   return r.value;
  case 'rolled_back': await storage.remove(key); throw r.error;
  case 'unknown':     throw r.cause;            // keep the bytes: the row may exist
  default:            return assertNever(r);
}
```

Routes declare what a constraint means and stop catching:

```ts
withOrgAllowed(ctx, request, fn, { constraints: {
  tag_org_scope_name_key: nameTaken,
  media_tag_tag_fk: () => notFound('The tag'),
} });
```

Writes now open their transaction through `withOrgAllowed(ctx, request, fn, options)` (`apps/api/src/auth/permissions.ts:454-471`), which already maps one SQLSTATE in one place (`55P03` to 503 with `retry-after`, `:424-445`); the constraint map is the `constraints` option of `withOrgAllowed`, not a new opener, and its private `settle` (`:425`) needs another name beside the exported `settle()`. An unmapped constraint stays a 500 and is logged by name. `CONSTRAINTS` becomes one object keyed by name (`ConstraintName = keyof typeof CONSTRAINTS`), and `packages/db/test/constraint-registry.integration.test.ts` asserts its keys equal the foreign keys, unique constraints, unique indexes and guard names of the migrated catalogue.

Lint for `apps/api/src/routes/**` and `publishing.ts`:

```js
{ selector: 'Literal[value=/^(23|40|42|55|57)[0-9A-Z]{3}$/]',
  message: 'Do not read SQLSTATE codes in a route. Add the constraint to packages/db/src/constraints.ts and map it in the constraints option of withOrgAllowed(ctx, request, fn, { constraints: { name: () => error } }).' },
{ selector: "BinaryExpression[operator='instanceof'][right.name='CommitOutcomeUnknownError']",
  message: "Use settle() from @tinnox/db and handle 'committed', 'rolled_back' and 'unknown'. Only 'rolled_back' may delete files or stored objects (SCX-03)." },
```

plus `no-restricted-imports` for `pgErrorCode` and `violatedConstraint` in routes. Flags today: 26 literals and one `instanceof` site (`routes/media.ts:343`). The other two, `media/pipeline.ts:674` and `scripts/provision-member.ts:82`, are outside those files, so the `instanceof` rule needs `apps/api/src/**` and `scripts/**` to see them. Step 7 moves `isUnknownTag` (`routes/tags.ts:90`, which reads `23503`) out of `routes/` (D6), so the literal rule follows it to its new module, `apps/api/src/tags.ts` since step 7; the classifier's `55P03` (`auth/permissions.ts:411`) and tests are exempt.

Also: an explicit handler branch for `CommitOutcomeUnknownError` with a unit test, one integration test of a real refusal at `COMMIT`, the same classification in the migration runner, and the two e-mail senders on `settle()`, mailing only in the `committed` arm, each with a lost-`COMMIT` test through `losingCommitAnswers`. On the wire (D9): POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE answer 500 with the new code `commit_outcome_unknown`, no `Retry-After`, `no-store`, and one fixed message, "The change may have been saved, but the server could not confirm it. Reload to check before you try again."; GET and HEAD keep `internal_error`. Only the cause's name and SQLSTATE are logged, and no e-mail is sent on an unknown outcome.

It cannot force a caller to choose `settle()`, and it does not cover autocommit statements. **Unverified:** whether `pg`'s error `table` field tells the two directions of one foreign key apart (`constraints.ts:22-26` documents `display_assigned_playlist_fk` as meaning two things); the classifier needs that to separate "reference missing" from "still in use".

### 2.4 Database access outside `withOrg`

**Census** (`apps/api/src`, tests excluded): 42 `withOrg` call sites plus the ones inside `withOrgDecision` and `withOrgAllowed`; 32 `withOrgAllowed`; 3 `withOrgDecisionAllowed`; no caller of `withOrgDecision`; 2 `withoutOrg`; 4 `withResolvedOrg`; 6 `poolAsTx`. Of 169 `.query` calls, 164 are on a transaction handle. The five that are not: `routes/ops.ts:29` (`ctx.pool.query('SELECT 1')`), `routes/player.ts:528` and `media/pipeline.ts:355` (raw `sec.*` SQL on a `poolAsTx` handle, with no wrapper in `sec.ts`), and `alerts.ts:78`, `:135` (the alert check's session advisory lock and unlock, on a connection taken from the pool at `alerts.ts:71`). The new `sec.*` calls (`sec.session_revoke_membership`, `sec.display_alert_orgs`, `sec.alert_recipient_by_token_hash`) all go through wrappers in `sec.ts` (`:212`, `:440`, `:453`). `apps/media-worker/src` has no database access at all. Outside `apps/`, `scripts/lib/provision.ts` uses `withOrg`, `withoutOrg` and `withResolvedOrg`, one direct pool query (`:375`) and `SET LOCAL ROLE tinnox_definer` (`:391`, `:575`).

**Proposal.**

```ts
declare const scope: unique symbol;                                   // not exported
export type OrgId = string & { readonly [scope]: 'org' };            // minted only by sec.ts lookups
export interface OrgTx    { readonly [scope]: 'change'; query: Tx['query'] }
export interface PreOrgTx { readonly [scope]: 'pre'; enterOrg(id: OrgId): Promise<OrgTx> }   // no query()
```

- `OrgId` is returned by the session, device, pairing-completion, job, alert-organisation and alert-confirmation lookups in `sec.ts` and by nothing else, so a request field cannot reach `withOrg` (rule AG-2.3). Two wrappers are added for the raw calls: `deviceByRegistrationId` and `mediaProcessingJobs`. Scripts get two minting paths (D25): `newOrgId` for the organisation `org-create` makes (`scripts/lib/provision.ts:488-489`) and for the seed, and the migrator-only definer helper in `@tinnox/db/migrate`, which reads as `tinnox_definer` and mints the `OrgId` that five provisioning commands pass to `enterOrg` (`scripts/lib/provision.ts:679`, `:823`, `:895`, `:968`, `:1025`).
- `pairingClaim`, `pairingCancelClaims`, `sessionRevokeMembership`, `lockOrgMembership`, `membershipForCheck` and the scoped audit take `OrgTx`. Today a wrong handle is found only when the database raises `42501` (`db/migrations/0010_pairing_hardening.sql:36-45`, redefined at `0013_roles_and_members.sql:429`; `sec.session_revoke_membership` at `0013_roles_and_members.sql:579-590`), which the API turns into a 404, or when `lockOrgMembership` throws on a missing organisation (`packages/db/src/members.ts:20-22`).
- A pre-organisation handle has no public `query`. The tenant read at `routes/player.ts:326` and the tenant update at `routes/alert-recipients.ts:187`, inside `withResolvedOrg`, move to the `OrgTx` that `enterOrg` returns.
- 33 `tx: Tx` helper parameters in 14 files change their annotation; call sites infer.
- `ping(pool)` in `packages/db` replaces `routes/ops.ts:29`, and a session-lock helper there replaces the alert check's own connection (`alerts.ts:71-151`).
- Two runtime guards in `run()`: mark the handle closed before release so a late `query` throws (U-2), and refuse a nested transaction with `AsyncLocalStorage` (U-3).
- Last, the pool goes private: `createDatabase()` returns an opaque handle with `readOrg`, `changeOrg`, `changePreOrg`, `auditStandalone` and `ping`; `AppContext.pool` becomes `db`. Tests keep the pool through a `@tinnox/db/testing` entry: 52 pool references in tests, including the fault-injection proxy, need that seam.

Lint that backs it (messages in section 3.4): no `.query` on anything but a transaction handle; no raw `sec.<name>(` string outside `sec.ts`; no `pg` import in `apps/*/src`; `withoutOrg` and `poolAsTx` importable only from today's six files in `apps/api/src` (and `scripts/lib/provision.ts`) until they are gone; no `as OrgTx`, `as Tx` or `as OrgId` outside `packages/db`.

### 2.5 Other places where an API removes a class

| Class | Change | Removes |
|---|---|---|
| SCX-02 | `#pending()` returns `Listing<T>` with `complete: true \| false`; the sweep runs only in the `complete` branch | a capped list reaching a delete loop |
| CU-02 | `publishExchangeFile(path, data)` on each side (write, chmod, rename) | umask deciding a cross-uid contract |
| AG-1.2 | `createPool` asserts `tinnox_app` itself | a second pool that skips the check |
| AG-4.12 | the runner sends `lock_timeout` | a role default deciding the limit |
| DEC-23 | the publication insert takes a branded `PlannedPublication` | a publication written without the capability check |
| LYX-03 | `planLayoutPublication` applies the predicate of `checkLayoutZones` (`packages/contracts/src/layout.ts:29`) and returns issues | a 500 from the manifest builder |
| LYX-02 | the player session exposes one `wake()` for the main loop and every zone | a caller forgetting the zones |
| LYX-01 | a pure `layoutBoxes(frame, views, stage)` | untestable layout in the DOM |
| FCX-03, FCX-04 | `useDraftSave`, called by the page; `EditorPage` requires its result | per-editor busy flags and leave guards |
| FCX-06 | `PlayerHttpApi` holds a not-before time per route | each loop threading `retryAfterMs` by hand |
| FCU-03 | `<Truncate>` sets `title` from its text | an ellipsis without the full value |
| SCX-05 | `withCountedLimit(tx, key, countSql, max)` | a count without its lock |
| Credential lookup window (section 4.7, D8) | No `withDevice` and no lock: the window is stated instead. The heartbeat's write takes effect only while its registration is still active (D8) | a late heartbeat from a replaced player hiding the new player's report |
| SCR-34 | `changeOrg(org, permit, fn)` takes a `WritePermit` that only the role guard creates (`apps/api/src/auth/permissions.ts:337`) and re-checks the role as its first statement; `readOrg` is read-only | a write that skips the role re-check; retires the per-file count in `apps/api/src/auth/write-guard.test.ts` |
| RCX-02, RFX-01 | `sec.session_revoke_membership` locks the member row itself, then stamps `clock_timestamp()` (a new migration); the incident script calls it | a cut-off stamped before the lock it depends on; five callers are correct only by statement order today (**unverified**: that the extra row lock adds no deadlock) |
| EAX-06 | `withSessionLock(pool, key, fn(signal))` in `packages/db`: a dedicated connection whose loss aborts the signal | work continuing after its session lock is lost; also removes the raw `.query` calls in `apps/api/src/alerts.ts` |
| EAX-08 | `isLoopbackAddress(host)` in `apps/api/src/config.ts`, literal addresses only | a security exemption decided on a host name (`config.ts:182-183` still does, outside production) |
| EAX-07 | one `patchOf(saved, draft)` helper for forms, the only producer of the branded `ChangedFields<T>` the client's `patch` requires; a required `expectedUpdatedAt` on the playlist, schedule and layout PUTs, checked after the row is locked (D24) | a stale tab writing back fields it did not change; four hand-written diff builders and the Canvas tab's full patch (`apps/portal/src/lib/screenDetailCanvas.ts:62-69`) today; a whole-document save over a newer one |
| SCR-11, UTX-04 | `<WriteErrorNotice>` in `apps/portal/src/ui/` chooses "could not be confirmed" from `isOutcomeUnknown` and reloads; the upload handle settles a discriminated `refused` or `unknown` outcome (D21) | a lost answer reported as a refusal; about 14 write dialogs by search, one read and confirmed; unknown uploads counted as "not uploaded" |
| RCX-01 class, alert addresses | one cut-off predicate in `apps/api/src/routes/alert-recipients.ts`, used by the confirm `UPDATE` and the list's status (D22) | a pending address confirmed after the person who added it was removed, demoted or signed out everywhere |
| EAX-14 | `ConfirmDialog.returnFocus` becomes required | focus falling to the page after a row is removed; twelve `ConfirmDialog` sites pass none today; four are row deletes (layouts, schedules, apps, folders). The prop becomes required with an explicit value (`'opener'` or a function), so the eight that remove no row state `'opener'` (**unverified pending a render**) |
| SCR-12 | the only exported person-name function takes the id (`memberLabel`) | two unnamed people who cannot be told apart |
| UTX-05 | `consumeUrlMarker(name)`: the address changes through the router, or before the router exists | the router and the address bar disagreeing; three native `replaceState` sites |
| UTX-06 | `useAnnounce()` writes into regions that stay mounted; `Banner` loses `live` | a live region mounted already filled (waits for the listening pass, CMS-6) |
| SCR-18 to SCR-20 | message tables typed `{ fact, action }`, rendered by a component that drops `action` when the role cannot act | an instruction shown to a role that cannot follow it |

| EAX-09 | the e2e API's environment is built from an allow-list, and the stack refuses to start unless every host and URL in it is loopback (`tests/e2e/support/stack.ts:90`, `:96`) | a test reaching a service the developer configured |

## 3. Layer 2: static checks

### 3.1 Import-direction boundaries

**The real graph**, built by resolving every import specifier in the 633 tracked code files, not from the manifests:

| Workspace | Imports from `@tinnox/*` |
|---|---|
| `packages/contracts`, `packages/storage`, `packages/db` | none (zod; `node:*`; `pg`) |
| `packages/domain`, `packages/app-render` | `contracts` |
| `packages/delivery` | `contracts`, `domain` |
| `apps/api` | `contracts`, `db`, `storage`, `domain`, `delivery` |
| `apps/media-worker` | `contracts` (not declared in its `package.json`; step 9 declared it) |
| `apps/portal`, `apps/player-web` | `contracts`, `domain`, `app-render` |
| `tests` | `db`, `contracts`, `domain`, `app-render`, plus 4 relative imports into `apps/api` (since step 7, `@tinnox/api/test-support`) and 2 into `scripts/lib` |
| `scripts` (not a workspace) | `db`, `contracts`, `domain`, and `pg` directly in `scripts/seed.ts:16` and `scripts/provision-member.ts:9` (both connect as `tinnox_migrator`; since step 5a neither imports `pg`) |

It is already a clean layering: no package imports an app, and no app imports an app. Almost none of it is enforced. The only boundary lint is the `packages/domain` block (`eslint.config.js:63-100`). Since step 7, one table, `IMPORT_LAYERS` in `eslint.config.js`, generates every `no-restricted-imports` block, the domain block folded in. npm hoists every workspace, so the type-checker resolves `@tinnox/db`, `pg` and `fastify` from `apps/portal/src/App.tsx`, and `@tinnox/api/src/app.ts` from `packages/contracts` (the apps have no `exports` map).

| Candidate rule | Violations today |
|---|---|
| (a) packages never import apps | 0 |
| (b) apps never import apps | 0 |
| (c) no reaching into another workspace by relative path or deep import | 4: `tests/integration/support.ts:12`, `:14`; `tests/integration/credential-audit.test.ts:11`; `tests/integration/reauth.test.ts:14`. Not counted, because `scripts` is not a workspace: `tests/integration/provision-member.test.ts:17`, `:18` import `../../scripts/lib/`. Fixed in step 7; the sketch's regex also missed a path between two workspaces with the same parent (see below the sketch) |
| (d) `packages/domain` imports only `@tinnox/contracts`, no platform API | 0. The existing rule is a deny-list with gaps: it misses `os`, `url`, `util`, `timers`, `zod`, `console`, `setTimeout`, `crypto`, `Buffer`, `Date.now()`, argument-less `new Date()`, `Math.random()` |
| (e) `packages/contracts` imports only zod | 0; no lint block covers it |
| (f) browser source never imports `pg`, `fastify`, `node:*`, `@tinnox/db`, `@tinnox/storage`, `@tinnox/delivery` | 0 |
| (g) only `packages/db` imports `pg`; only `apps/api` imports `fastify` | 2: `scripts/seed.ts:16`, `scripts/provision-member.ts:9`, both migrator clients by design; step 5a moves both behind `connectMigrator` (D4). 0 since step 5a |
| (h) non-test files never import test files | 0, provided the component setup file `apps/portal/src/componentTests.setup.ts` counts as a test file (it imports `testFrame.test-support.ts`) |
| (i) route files do not import each other; only `app.ts` imports from `routes/` | 11 (for example `apps/api/src/routes/playlists.ts:56-57`, `overview.ts:18-20`, `apps/api/src/publishing.ts:55-56`, and the test import `apps/api/src/security.test.ts:10`). Adopted in full, tests included (D6): the 18 shared exports of five route files move out of `routes/` in step 7, with no allow-list. Done in step 7: they live in `apps/api/src/display-state.ts`, `app-rows.ts`, `tags.ts`, `media/rows.ts` and `media/upload-name.ts`, and `loadPushPlan` and `pendingCount` are in `publishing.ts` |

**Recommendation: generated `no-restricted-imports` blocks from one table in `eslint.config.js`.** It is the pattern the repository already uses, needs no dependency, and the rule accepts regular expressions. Two limits to design around: flat config **replaces** a rule's options when two blocks match one file, so all blocks are generated from one table and the domain block is folded in; and the rule does not see dynamic `import()` or `require()`. As built, the outbound rule has generated load selectors for them; every other boundary is held by a unit test over files (`scripts/lib/import-boundaries-lint.test.ts`, with `scripts/lib/load-specifiers.ts`) that lints each literal dynamic specifier as a static import against the file's own config (review fix LINT-1). A computed specifier is left to review.

```js
const only = (...ok) => `^@tinnox/(?!(${ok.join('|') || '~'})(/|$))`;
const SHARED = [
  { regex: '^@tinnox/(api|portal|player-web|media-worker|tests)(/|$)',
    message: 'Apps are not libraries. Move the shared code into a package under packages/ and import that.' },
  { regex: '^\\.{1,2}/(.*/)?(apps|packages)/[a-z-]+/(src|test|sw|build)(/|$)',
    message: "Do not reach into another workspace by relative path. Import '@tinnox/<name>' and export what you need from its src/index.ts." },
];
const LAYERS = {                       // allowed @tinnox packages per workspace
  'packages/contracts': [],
  'packages/db': [],                   // empty until step 17 opens the type-only contracts import (D19)
  'packages/domain': ['contracts'],
  'packages/delivery': ['contracts', 'domain'],
  'apps/api': ['contracts', 'domain', 'delivery', 'storage', 'db'],
  'apps/portal': ['contracts', 'domain', 'app-render'],
  tests: ['contracts', 'db', 'domain', 'app-render', 'api/test-support'],
  scripts: ['db', 'contracts', 'domain'],
  // one line per workspace
};
```

As built in step 7, the table is `IMPORT_LAYERS` in `eslint.config.js`, one row per workspace plus `scripts`, and the relative-path entry has a second alternative, `^(\.\./)+(<workspace leaf>)/(src|test|sw|build)(/|$)`, with the leaves generated from the table: the regex above misses a sibling path such as `../../api/src/app.ts` between two workspaces with the same parent.

Extra patterns for non-test files: in `packages/domain`, `^(?!@tinnox/contracts$|\./)` ("packages/domain is pure: take time, ids and I/O results as parameters"); in `packages/contracts`, `^(?!zod(/|$)|\./)`; in `packages/db/src/**`, `@tinnox/contracts` with `allowTypeImports`, so only `import type` passes (D19; step 9's manifest test then wants `@tinnox/contracts` under `packages/db` `dependencies`, which step 17 adds); in browser source, `^(node:|pg(/|$)|fastify(/|$)|@fastify/|@tinnox/(db|storage|delivery)(/|$))` ("This file is bundled for the browser. Call the API through apps/portal/src/api or apps/player-web/src/platform/api.ts."); outside `packages/db`, `^pg(/|$)` ("Only packages/db talks to PostgreSQL. Use withOrg and the sec.* wrappers from '@tinnox/db'."), with no exception, because step 5a moves the two migrator clients of rule (g) behind `connectMigrator` in `packages/db` (D4, D25). Rule (i), two entries (D6): in `apps/api/src/routes/**`, `^\./|(^|/)routes/`, so neither a sibling import nor a path through `routes/` passes; elsewhere in `apps/api/**`, except `src/app.ts`, `(^|/)routes/`. The rule compares the specifier text and never resolves it, which is why the route-file entry needs both halves. As built, "browser source" is the browser files minus tests and minus `vite.config.ts` (those run in Node), and bare built-ins are banned by a fixed list of server-only names (`NODE_ONLY_MODULES`), not by every name Node has, so browser polyfills such as `buffer` stay importable. Product code may not import `scripts/lib` by relative path (review fix LINT-12).

A dry run of the layer table over 631 files reported exactly the 4 lines of rule (c), once the `scripts` line allows `contracts` and `domain` (without it, `scripts/seed.ts:15` and `scripts/lib/provision.ts:21`, `:41` are reported too). They are fixed by adding `"exports": { "./test-support": "./test/support.ts" }` to `apps/api/package.json`, re-exporting the `IdentityProvider` type from that module, and importing `@tinnox/api/test-support`. Step 7 made exactly this fix.

Alternatives considered: `eslint-plugin-boundaries` (a plugin and a resolver; not installed, compatibility with ESLint 10 **unverified**), dependency-cruiser (a second tool whose errors stay out of the editor; worth revisiting for cycles), TypeScript project references (need `composite` and declaration output in a `noEmit` repository, and say nothing about third-party imports).

Two companions:

- **Manifest drift.** A unit test `scripts/lib/workspace-deps.test.ts` compares each workspace's imports with its `package.json`. About a dozen mismatches today, for example `@tinnox/contracts` undeclared in `apps/media-worker/package.json`; `fastify-plugin` and `file-type` declared but never imported in `apps/api/package.json:26-27`; `pg` a runtime dependency of the API though only tests import it (`:30`). Whether the unused ones were deliberate was **unverified**. Step 9 found 12 entries, 13 with the allow-listed `axe-core` pin, and fixed them; none was deliberate except `axe-core` in `tests`, which fixes the rule engine `@axe-core/playwright` loads.
- **Type-level isolation.** `"types": []` in `packages/contracts/tsconfig.json` (zero errors) and `packages/domain/tsconfig.json` (two test lines to change: `packages/domain/src/playlist.test.ts:345`, `:459`), so Node globals do not type-check in pure packages. Done in step 7; the two test lines now count bytes with a local `utf8Bytes()` helper.

### 3.2 Banned patterns

Every row was searched at `002c3d6`; where a step built on the branch on 2026-11-22 changed a row, the row says so. "Fixes" is what must change in the pull request that turns the rule on.

| Pattern | Hits today | Rule | Fixes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `.query(` on anything but a transaction handle, in app source | 5 of 169 | `no-restricted-syntax` on the receiver | `ops.ts:29`, `player.ts:528`, `pipeline.ts:355`; the alert check's advisory lock on a dedicated connection, `alerts.ts:78`, `:135` (a lock helper in `packages/db` or an allow-list entry); the rule covers `scripts/**/*.ts` with no exception (D25) |
| `pg` imported outside `packages/db` (non-test) | 2 | `no-restricted-imports` | `scripts/seed.ts:16`, `scripts/provision-member.ts:9`: both move behind `connectMigrator` in step 5a, so the ban has no exception (D4, D25). 0 since step 5a; the ban landed in step 7 |
| `withoutOrg` / `poolAsTx` outside an allow-list | 7 files: six in `apps/api/src`, all reaching only `sec.*`, though four are not "before the organisation is known" (AG-2.2: purges, job discovery, sign-out fallback, `alerts.ts:84`); `scripts/lib/provision.ts:570`, the provisioning exception of `AGENTS.md:17` | `no-restricted-imports` with `importNames` | none; the allow-list records each file's reason |
| raw `sec.<name>(` SQL outside `sec.ts` | 2 | `no-restricted-syntax` on string and template text | two wrappers |
| organisation id read from the request | 0 | member-expression selector; contracts unit test over every request schema. Both built in step 10 (`NO_ORG_FROM_REQUEST`, `packages/contracts/src/org-id.test.ts`); neither sees an aliased or cast request object, so the `AGENTS.md` sentence stays until step 18 | none |
| 403 in a route file | 3 literals, all in `routes/player.ts`; role refusals already come from named constructors in `auth/permissions.ts:288-299` | section 2.2: a `new HttpError(` whose code has `STATUS_OF` 403, the code list derived from `STATUS_OF` in the config (after step 28 no status literal is passed, so a selector on 403 would stop matching), plus the import ban | move three constructors; `routes/members.ts:40` moves behind the people-route constructor, and `routes/tags.ts:22` goes with the tag-scope checks, which PATCH and DELETE all-of rules replace (D20) |
| SQLSTATE literal in a route file | 26 (24 of `23505`, `23503`, `23001`; 2 of `55P03`); since step 7, 25 in route files and one `23503` in `apps/api/src/tags.ts` | section 2.3 | eleven route files; the rule also covers `apps/api/src/tags.ts`, where `isUnknownTag` lives since step 7 (D6) |
| audit writer given an autocommit handle | 5 (among them `routes/auth.ts:273`, a failed sign-in recorded after the rollback) | the `Tx` type (section 2.1) | 5 |
| `UPDATE`/`DELETE` on `audit_event`, or its trigger changed | 0 in source, scripts and migrations | migration text check (step 5): `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `TRUNCATE` and `GRANT` on `audit_event`, and since review fix CHK-2 `DROP TABLE`, an `ALTER TABLE` that disables a trigger, enables a replica trigger or changes the owner, and `CREATE OR REPLACE`, `ALTER` or `DROP TRIGGER` on it; a new trigger and a new column pass (0004 and 0005 add one each). A statement inside a `DO` block or a function body, a replaced trigger function body and a policy on `audit_event` are not seen by the text check: step 6's catalogue test holds those | none |
| `INSERT INTO audit_event` text outside `packages/db/src/sec.ts`, scripts included (D25) | 1: `scripts/lib/provision.ts:582` | selector on string and template text | none; one named allow-list entry, provisioning's set-based expiry of pending invites |
| `SET ROLE` text outside the migrator-only definer helper in `@tinnox/db/migrate` (D25) | 2: `scripts/lib/provision.ts:391`, `:575` | selector on string and template text | the definer helper replaces provisioning's `asDefiner` (`scripts/lib/provision.ts:389-395`) |
| `process.env` outside the entry points and the config modules | 0 beyond those; the entry points are the two `main.ts` files and `apps/api/src/alerts-once.ts:16` | `no-restricted-properties`. Built in step 10: `ENV_READERS` names `apps/api/src/main.ts`, `apps/api/src/alerts-once.ts`, `apps/media-worker/src/main.ts` and `scripts/lib/env.ts`, and the scope also covers `scripts/lib/**` and `apps/player-web/sw/**` | none |

One `errorField` in `packages/db/src/error-field.ts` (exported from `@tinnox/db`) serves db and api; the other sites use the language's own narrowing |

| API read inside `useEffect` in portal features | 4 files; a selector on the call inside the effect sees 2 (`displays/wizard/useWizardScreen.ts:55`, `dashboard/useActivityNames.ts:109`), the other two call a loader defined outside the effect | selector on `api.get*`/`list*` inside `useEffect` | the wizard moves to `useLoad` |
