| `window.localStorage`, `insertAdjacentHTML`, `cssText` | 0 | extend the existing browser block (step 10; review fixes LINT-7 and LINT-8 added more sinks and the aliased and destructured storage forms) | none |
| `.skip`, `.only`, `.todo`, `.fixme`, `skipIf`, `runIf` in tests | 0 | selector on test files (section 4.1); built in step 2, also over `suite`, the chained forms and the options form | none |
| `vi.mock` in integration or e2e tests | 0 | `no-restricted-properties`; built in step 2, also for `doMock`, `importMock` and `stubGlobal` | none |
| `Math.random()` called in tests | 2: `apps/api/test/player.integration.test.ts:142`, `tests/integration/production-refusal.test.ts:97` (the 11 `Math.random` values passed in `apps/player-web/src/core/playback.test.ts` are not calls) | selector on the call | a seeded shuffle; a fixed or `crypto` value (step 2 used a fixed scrambled order and fixed secrets) |
| a status asserted as a range or a negation in tests (`>= 400`, `not.toBe(401)`) (RCX-03) | 14 sites (18 assertion lines; a `>= 400` / `< 500` pair counts once) | selector over `toBeGreaterThanOrEqual`, `toBeLessThan` and `.not.toBe` on a `.status`; an `expectError(response, code)` helper | the exact status and code at each site; four of the sites are in `tests/integration/cross-org.test.ts` (`:543-544`, `:684`, `:691`, `:702`) and are the section 2.2 conformance fix |
| `history.replaceState` / `pushState` in the portal outside one URL-marker module (UTX-05) | 3 | `no-restricted-properties` | `consumeUrlMarker` (section 2.5) |
| `role="status"`, `role="alert"` or `aria-live` in portal JSX outside `ui/` (UTX-06) | about 37, in 34 files | selector on `JSXAttribute` | `useAnnounce`; after the listening pass (CMS-6) |
| session advisory-lock SQL text (`pg_advisory_lock`, `pg_try_advisory_lock`, `pg_advisory_unlock`) outside `packages/db/src` (EAX-06) | 1 caller: `apps/api/src/alerts.ts:79`, `:135`. The five `pg_advisory_xact_lock` sites (`routes/tags.ts:160`, `routes/media.ts:290`, `routes/alert-recipients.ts:89`, `routes/folders.ts:81`, `publishing.ts:221`) are transaction locks and belong to `withCountedLimit` (SCX-05), not to this rule | selector on string text, restricted to the session-lock functions | `withSessionLock` |
| comparison with `'localhost'`, `'127.0.0.1'` or `'::1'` in `apps/api/src` outside `config.ts` (EAX-08) | 1: `apps/api/src/mail.ts:29`. Outside the rule: `apps/portal/src/lib/screenWizard.ts:328` and `apps/player-web/build/shell-plugin.mjs:46` compare a host name too, but to find the local player in development, not to relax a security setting | selector on `BinaryExpression` | `isLoopbackAddress` |
| "operator" in customer-facing portal text (SCR-17) | 1, not 0: the development-only styleguide used the demo name "Workshop Operator" (`apps/portal/src/features/styleguide/ActionSections.tsx`), renamed in step 10 | add to the status-wording selector | none |
| bare `userEvent.keyboard(` in component tests (SCR-16) | 2: `apps/portal/src/features/playlists/editor/PlaylistEditor.test.tsx:134`, and `apps/portal/src/testFrame.test-support.ts:21` (a modifier press; allow-list it or exclude `*.test-support.ts`) | selector | `userEvent.type(locator, ...)` (step 10; `testFrame.test-support.ts` has one named exception block) |
| `ErrorNotice` for a failed write (SCR-11) | about 14 files, by search | selector, once `WriteErrorNotice` exists | section 2.5 |
| the strings `MIGRATOR_URL`, `MIGRATOR_PASSWORD`, `POSTGRES_SUPERUSER`, `PROVISION_IDP_CLIENT_SECRET` in `apps/*/src` and `packages/*/src` (AG-1.9, AG-1.17); `MIGRATOR_URL` also in `scripts/**`, outside a named list of files (D4) | 0 | selector on literals and names. The override for `apps/api/src/config.ts`, whose refusal names them, lands with that refusal in step 12, not in step 10 (D5). `MIGRATOR_URL_READERS` lists `scripts/migrate.ts`, `scripts/seed.ts`, `scripts/provision-member.ts` and `scripts/setup-dev.mjs`; test files are outside the rule | none |
| `TODO`, `FIXME`, `XXX`, `HACK` comments | 0 | `no-warning-comments`; as built (step 10, review fix LINT-10) at the start of a comment only, so a marker fails and prose that mentions the word passes. Its message cannot be customised | none |
| secrets in log calls | 0 by a name heuristic over 57 logger calls | a table-driven test over the redaction paths in `apps/api/src/logger.ts:9-33` (the current test covers two fields; step 10 added one over every path) | none |

The evidence does **not** support a blanket ban on `useEffect` (65 uses in the portal), timers or comments. No finding traces to a raw `useEffect` or a timer in product code, and there are no `TODO` or hack comments and only five `eslint-disable` lines, each with a reason. It supports the narrow rules above.

### 3.3 Migrations are append-only and numbered

**What the runner validates** (`packages/db/src/migrate.ts`): the name pattern (`:19`, `:35-38`) and unique numbers (`:39`), since step 5 through the shared `validateMigrationNames`; `current_user` (`:51-54`; since step 5a `connectMigrator` checks `current_user` and `session_user`); an advisory lock (`:55`); applied files still present (`:69-72`); no new file sorting before the last applied one (`:80-84`); checksum equal (`:86-89`); one transaction per file with its history row (`:97-104`).

**What it cannot see:** a merged file edited, renamed or deleted before this database applied it; a gap; two branches taking the same number (seen only after they meet: the duplicate check runs before the runner connects, `migrate.ts:39`, so it then fails the integration job); a file that does not end in `.sql`; a `COMMIT` inside a file. CI is always a fresh database, so CI sees none of the first group.

**`scripts/check-migrations.ts`**, a step in the quality job, in the house style of `scripts/audit.ts` (a thin script, pure rules in `scripts/lib/migrations-check.ts`, unit tests beside it, which the unit project already picks up at `vitest.config.ts:25`):

1. Every entry of `db/migrations` matches `NNNN_description.sql`; numbers are unique and contiguous from 0001. The pure name check is extracted from `migrate.ts:32-41` so the runner and the script share it.
2. **Append-only**, in two modes (D12). On a branch or pull request, every migration at the merge-base of `HEAD` and `origin/main` is byte-identical in `HEAD`. On `main`, `git log --first-parent --diff-merges=first-parent --diff-filter=DMRT HEAD -- db/migrations` lists no commit outside `MAIN_HISTORY_EXCEPTIONS` in `scripts/lib/migrations-check.ts` (empty): the first-parent history may not delete, modify, rename or retype a migration. So `main` takes merge commits or squash merges only (D12). Message: "db/migrations/0007_stage_b_apps.sql is already on main and was modified. Merged migrations are immutable: every existing database rejects a changed checksum. Put the fix in db/migrations/NNNN_<description>.sql, with the next free number."
3. Added files number above the highest on `origin/main` (D12).
4. Text rules over each file, skipping dollar-quoted bodies and comments: no `CONCURRENTLY`, `VACUUM`, top-level `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`, `CREATE`/`ALTER`/`DROP ROLE`, UUID literals; `sec.*` function DDL only between `SET LOCAL ROLE tinnox_definer;` and `RESET ROLE;`; `UPDATE`/`DELETE`/`TRUNCATE`/`GRANT` on `audit_event` only with a marker line; a new file that alters an existing table carries a `-- Locks:` header. As built in step 5: the marker is the comment line `-- audit_event change: <why>` directly above the statement; the `-- Locks:` header is required from 0015 (`LOCKS_HEADER_FROM`); the rule also flags `SET ROLE` without `LOCAL`, a switch that is never reset, a switch to any role but `tinnox_definer`, `CREATE`, `ALTER` or `DROP` of a `USER` or `GROUP`, and `GRANT ... ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA public`. Review fix CHK-2 added `DROP TABLE`, the trigger-disabling and owner forms of `ALTER TABLE`, and `CREATE OR REPLACE`, `ALTER` or `DROP TRIGGER` on `audit_event`; a statement inside a `DO` block or a function body is still not seen, and step 6's catalogue test holds those.
5. Every file is listed in `db/upgrade-tests.json` with a test path or a reason. The file is sorted by file name, so two branches that add the same number conflict at merge (D12).

Needs: `fetch-depth: 0` on the quality job's checkout (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:28-30`; the secrets job already has it at `:79`), and a `.gitattributes` line `db/migrations/*.sql -text` so checksums survive a checkout with line-ending conversion (rule U-16). Both landed in step 5.

What it flags today: rule 4 flags 0002 (its 14 `CREATE FUNCTION sec.*` statements, from `db/migrations/0002_security_functions.sql:19`, have no role switch; ownership moves in the `DO` block at `:381-394`) and 0001 (the two `audit_event` grants at `0001_schema.sql:490` and `:498`). Grandfather both by name; neither file can change. 0013 and 0014 pass every rule-4 text rule (no hit for `CONCURRENTLY`, `VACUUM`, top-level transaction control, role DDL, UUID literals or `audit_event` statements; their `sec.*` DDL sits inside the switch). Rule 5 needs fourteen entries: test paths for 0005, 0012, 0013 and 0014; reasons, or the shared test path (`migration-0005.integration.test.ts:544`), for 0001 to 0004 and 0006 to 0011, with 0010's saying what 0012 later repaired.

**Decided (D12).** Git is the authority, and there is no checksum manifest: a manifest proves consistency, not immutability (editing both passes), and the sorted `db/upgrade-tests.json` gives the merge conflict it would have given. The check depends neither on branch protection nor on branches being up to date. Until a ruleset on `main` exists it reports but does not block a merge, and a force push defeats the history rule (section 7, "Still needs Michael"). Backstop: the runner's duplicate check (`migrate.ts:39`). It does depend on how `main` is merged: merge commits or squash merges only. A fast-forward push or a rebase merge puts a branch's own commits on the first-parent history, so a review fix to the branch's new migration reads as a change to a merged one (review finding CHK-1). The check's message for a non-merge commit explains this; the remedy is an entry in `MAIN_HISTORY_EXCEPTIONS` with its reason, never restoring bytes that were not on `main`.

**Move the guards into the runner (Layer 1).** Twelve of fourteen migrations paste 20 `DO` blocks of three kinds: forced RLS (0001, 0005, 0007, 0009, 0011, 0013, 0014), `sec.*` hardening (0002, 0003, 0004, 0010, 0012, 0013, 0014) and, since 0005, a tenant policy on each new table plus no disabled trigger in `public` (`0005_portal_v2_stage_a.sql:299`, `0007_stage_b_apps.sql:92`, `0009_stage_b_schedules.sql:203`, `0011_stage_b_layouts.sql:183`, `0013_roles_and_members.sql:624`, `0014_display_alerts.sql:168`). The `sec.*` copy already exists in three variants: 0002 to 0004 omit the `EXECUTE` checks, and every copy before 0014 passes a definer function with no `SET` clause, which only 0014's corrects (`0014_display_alerts.sql:192-205`). One `assertSchemaInvariants(client)` called by the runner after each file and before its `COMMIT` replaces them for every future migration; it must keep the `schema_migrations` exemption (`migrate.ts:56-61`). Settled by reading that it holds after each historical migration: every file that creates a table (0001, 0005, 0007, 0009, 0011, 0013, 0014) ends with the forced-RLS guard, every file that touches `sec` functions ends with the `sec.*` guard, 0006 and 0008 create neither, and FORCE is lifted only inside 0005 (`:185-201`) and 0013 (`:71-85`) and restored before the guard. A from-scratch run is still the proof.

### 3.4 Custom ESLint rules and their messages

**Where they live.** `scripts/eslint-rules/`, in TypeScript, one file per rule, built with `RuleCreator` (D16). `scripts/tsconfig.json` type-checks it, `eslint.config.js:57` lints it, and the unit project runs `scripts/**/*.test.ts` (precedent: `scripts/lib/audit-check.test.ts`). Rules are tested with ESLint's `RuleTester` and the TypeScript parser, and an `index.test.ts` fails when a rule has no test or is enabled in no config block. Loading a `.ts` plugin relies on Node's type stripping, so `eslint.config.js` first throws a clear error unless `process.features.typescript` is set, and only then loads the plugin with `await import()`, never a static import, which would load before the guard runs. `engines.node` rises to `>=22.18.0` in `package.json` and the lockfile; `engines` is advisory (`.npmrc` has no `engine-strict`), so the guard is what holds the floor. The plugin lands with the first custom rule, step 13's nested-transaction lint. The CI run recorded in `docs/security/control-matrix.md` (B12) used Node v22.23.4, which strips types by default (22.18 and later); unverified: that every later run resolves 22.18 or newer (`.nvmrc` names `22`). The secret scan already depends on type stripping since the step 1 follow-up: `npm run scan:secrets` runs `node --experimental-strip-types`, a flag the script can drop once step 13 raises `engines.node`.

**Most rules need no plugin.** `no-restricted-syntax`, `no-restricted-imports`, `no-restricted-properties` and `no-restricted-globals` cover all but three. Because a later block replaces an earlier block's options for the same rule (the portal block at `eslint.config.js:136-143` already owns a `no-restricted-syntax` array), compose **one array per file group** from named selector lists. Test each selector against fixture text with `ESLint#lintText` in a unit test; do not trust a selector by reading (the inline-cast selector of section 3.2 misses `as { ... } | null` unless the union case is added). The repository already has one rule of this kind written as a regular-expression count over route files, `apps/api/src/auth/write-guard.test.ts:21-58`; the legacy-transaction rule below would replace it. As built in steps 2 to 10, `eslint.config.js` composes named lists (`PRODUCT_SYNTAX`, `API_SYNTAX`, `NO_NON_NULL` and the others) per file group, and each rule has fixture cases in `scripts/lib/*-lint.test.ts`.

Every message names the API to use instead:

| Rule | Message |
|---|---|
| no pool query | "Do not call query() on the pool: the statement runs outside any transaction and with no organisation. Use withOrg(pool, session.orgId, (tx) => ...), a typed sec.* wrapper from @tinnox/db, or ping(pool) for a liveness probe." |
| `sec.*` only in `sec.ts` | "Call sec.* through a typed wrapper in packages/db/src/sec.ts; add the wrapper if it is missing." |
| unscoped helpers | "These run without a tenant. Use withOrg(pool, session.orgId, fn). If the organisation really is not known yet, add this file to the allow-list in eslint.config.js and say why in the PR." |
| organisation id from the request | "The organisation comes from operatorOf(request).orgId or the device credential, never from the request." |
| no `pg` in apps | "Only packages/db talks to PostgreSQL. Use createPool and withOrg from @tinnox/db; a direct pg client skips the tinnox_app role check and the row_security and timeout start-up parameters." |
| clock | "Do not read the clock here: use the injected clock, ctx.now() (AppContext.now in apps/api/src/context.ts), or take a `now` option or parameter from the caller, as MediaPipelineOptions.now and runDisplayAlerts({ now }) do, so tests can set the time. Only main.ts and alerts-once.ts create the clock." (as built in step 10: the media pipeline has no `ctx`) |
| SQL fragments (custom) | "SQL text may interpolate only module constants or a SqlFragment. Pass values as parameters ($1, $2); see Tx in packages/db/src/tx.ts." |
| nested transaction (custom) | "This opens a second transaction on another connection that commits independently. Pass the outer tx to the helper." |
| legacy transaction API (custom, with an allow-list that only shrinks) | "Use db.readOrg(orgId, fn) for reads and db.changeOrg(orgId, permit, fn) returning { value, audit } for changes; the permit comes from the role guard." |
| inline-object cast | "Do not cast to an inline object type to reach a member the declared type lacks. Add it to the interface (as ObjectStorage.removeStaleTempFiles, CX-04) or narrow with a type guard." |
| exchange file mode | "The mode option is reduced by the process umask (CU-02). Use publishExchangeFile() or makeExchangeDir(), which chmod explicitly." |
| text keys | "Key a list by the row id, or by index for plain strings from the API. Screen and playlist names are not unique and React drops rows with equal keys (LYX-04)." |
| read inside `useEffect` | "Read through useLoad (hooks/useLoad.ts): it aborts on unmount and setData discards reads in flight (FCX-05)." |
| raw dialog | "Use <Dialog> from ui/: a raw <dialog> lets Tab leave the modal (FCU-06)." |
| `fetch` | "Call the server through the API client (apps/portal/src/api/client.ts or PlayerHttpApi): its deadline covers the body (FCX-01) and it keeps Retry-After (FCX-06)." |
| outbound call | "Outbound calls need a data-location review (DEC-13, DEC-15). The approved clients are auth/oidc.ts (identity provider) and mail.ts (SMTP, DEC-37)." |
| skipped test | "A skipped, focused or expected-to-fail test is a red run (U-15): it hides a failure, or every other test. Fix the test or delete it in this change; there is no allow-list for a declared skip." (as built in step 2: the lint cannot read `tests/skip-allowlist.json`, and pointing authors at it would lead to disable comments; that file governs runtime results only, in step 3's results gate) |
| mock in integration | "Integration and e2e tests use real services (tests/README.md). Inject a fault through the pool wrapper in apps/api/test/support.ts instead." |
| status wording | "Status wording stays honest (P2-04): say what the player last reported; use the summaries in lib/screenStatus.ts." |

As built in steps 2, 7 and 10, the pg, outbound, mock and status-wording messages are longer than these sketches, and the pg and mock messages change with the file group so each names only what that group may use (review fix LINT-3); the text lives in `eslint.config.js`. Some messages still name no alternative: `no-warning-comments` keeps ESLint's fixed message; three older browser entries say only "dangerouslySetInnerHTML is not allowed.", "Do not assign HTML strings." (`outerHTML`) and "Do not use document.write."; and review fix LINT-7 added "Do not use document.writeln." in the same form.

### 3.5 Type-checker and lint strictness

All 14 `tsconfig` programs resolve to the same flags: `strict`, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `noImplicitOverride`, `noFallthroughCasesInSwitch`, `noImplicitReturns`, `verbatimModuleSyntax`, `erasableSyntaxOnly`. Type-check and type-aware lint reach every `.ts` and `.tsx` file, tests and scripts included. Outside both: ten `.mjs`/`.js` files (2,557 lines), SQL, shell and the CI workflow.

| Change | Cost today |
|---|---|
| `@typescript-eslint/switch-exhaustiveness-check` | 3 sites: `apps/player-web/src/core/machine.ts:128`, `apps/portal/src/lib/activity.ts:399`, `apps/portal/src/lib/screenDetail.ts:319`. It is what makes the closed unions of sections 2.1 and 2.3 bite. Built in step 8 with `considerDefaultExhaustiveForUnions: false`; `screenDetail.ts:319` switches on `string \| null`, so only the other two are closed unions |
| no non-null assertion outside tests | 0. Built in step 8 as a `no-restricted-syntax` entry on `TSNonNullExpression` (`NO_NON_NULL`), not as `@typescript-eslint/no-non-null-assertion`, whose fixed message cannot name what to do instead (section 3.4). Neither rule was on before step 8 |
| `types: []` for `contracts` and `domain` | 0 and 2 test lines; done in step 7 |
| `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` | 56 errors: 55 in the portal, 1 in `apps/api/test/support.ts:875`. Worth it later, not now |
| the whole `strictTypeChecked` preset | not recommended: one rule alone (`restrict-template-expressions`) adds 526 reports |

### 3.6 Catalogue tests

These need a database, so they run in the integration job, but they are static in spirit: they read the schema after all migrations and fail on a shape, not on a behaviour. They replace hand-written lists with enumeration. One new file, `packages/db/test/catalogue.integration.test.ts`; each query must return no row. Each query exempts `public.schema_migrations`, which the runner creates without row-level security (`packages/db/src/migrate.ts:56-61`), as the existing test and guards do (`packages/db/test/rls.integration.test.ts:132`). No catalogue-wide assertion has been added since: the 0013 and 0014 tests pin their own tables only, and each new table was typed into three hand lists (`rls.integration.test.ts:20-46`, `migration-0005.integration.test.ts:598-634`, `tests/integration/cross-org.test.ts:730-755`).

| Assertion | Replaces | Expected today |
|---|---|---|
| exact attributes of the three roles; exact membership set | AG-1.1, AG-1.5 | passes (**unverified**); the membership half lands first, in step 5a: `tinnox_app` is a member of no role, and the migrator's only membership is `tinnox_definer` without inherit (D4). As built, that is a case in `packages/db/test/rls.integration.test.ts` that filters on the member side only; this assertion filters both sides, so a grant to `tinnox_definer` fails too (SEC-6) |
| nothing is owned by `tinnox_app`; everything in `public` by `tinnox_migrator`; `tinnox_definer` owns only functions in `sec`; no definer function outside `sec` | AG-1.4, AG-1.8, AG-1.12 | passes (**unverified**) |
| every privilege of `tinnox_app`, `tinnox_definer` and `PUBLIC` equals `catalogue/grants.json` | AG-1.6, AG-1.7, AG-1.18 | the snapshot is generated in the same pull request; it subsumes the exact lists for nine tables (AG-1.6). Message: "tinnox_app gained UPDATE on public.media_tag. If the feature needs it, update grants.json in the same PR and say why in the migration header." |
| every tenant table has one `ALL` policy on `app_org_id()`; the tenant list is derived, not typed | AG-2.5, AG-2.8 | fixes the `app` omission in `cross-org.test.ts` (still absent from `:730-755`); needs fixture rows for the five tables `createOrg` leaves empty (`rls.integration.test.ts:53-61`: `player_registration`, `heartbeat_log`, `member_invite`, `display_alert`, `alert_recipient`) |
| every foreign key into a tenant table carries `org_id` | AG-2.6 | one allow-list entry: `pairing_claim_failure.membership_id` (the foreign keys added by 0013 and 0014 are all composite) |
| triggers and grants on `audit_event` are exactly the two triggers (`audit_event_no_update`, `audit_event_stamp`), `INSERT`, `SELECT` for `tinnox_app` and `INSERT` for `tinnox_definer` (`db/migrations/0001_schema.sql:490`, `:498`) | AG-3.6, AG-3.7 | passes (**unverified**); the `tinnox_app` table-level part is already asserted (`migration-0013.integration.test.ts:431-433`) |
| the `sec` function register (`name -> { crossOrg, why }`) equals the catalogue | AG-1.16 | 21 entries to write |
| no `sec` function body names `member_invite` | AG-1.19 | passes (by reading the migrations) |
| every CHECK over a nullable column or JSON path is NULL-safe or allow-listed | CX-02 | about 30 allow-list entries (**estimate**), plus seven from 0013 and 0014 that pass on `NULL` by design (`0013_roles_and_members.sql:51-52`, `:201-204`; `0014_display_alerts.sql:80`) |
| the constraint registry equals the catalogue; guard names raised by triggers (for example `membership_last_owner`, `db/migrations/0013_roles_and_members.sql:139`) are read from function bodies, because they are not in `pg_constraint` | U-9 | at least 17 names to add (section 2.3) |
| the `sec` register also pins each function's result type (`pg_get_function_result`) | EAX-11, AG-1.14, AG-1.20 | 21 entries |

---

## 4. Layer 3: verification gaps

The test set-up is stricter than most: retries are 0, `forbidOnly` is on, there is no declared skip anywhere, and the e2e global setup throws when a service is missing (`tests/e2e/global-setup.ts:19-21`, `tests/e2e/support/stack.ts:82-86`, `:118`, `:143-154`). Nearly all of that is convention. Useful harness pieces already exist and should be generalised, not reinvented: `losingCommitAnswers`, `statementHolds`, `whileAuditFails`, `twoTransactions` and `stillWaiting`, and the build-at-level code copied into five upgrade tests.

### 4.1 e2e must fail on skipped specs

**Today.** No skip, todo, fixme or only exists in any suite (searches in section 8). The 104 `test(` sites in the 16 spec files of `tests/e2e` expand to 150 tests through loops (`npx playwright test -c tests/playwright.config.ts --list`). If one were skipped:

- Playwright would exit 0 (**unverified by a run**; read from the reporter code). The JSON reporter counts a declared skip and a serial "did not run" under the same `skipped` total.
- Nothing reads `tests/.run/e2e-results.json`. It is written (`tests/playwright.config.ts:27`) and uploaded only on failure (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:115-120`).
- Fourteen of the sixteen spec files run in serial mode (all but `03-isolation` and `05-hardening`; in `09-review-round-2.spec.ts` only the portal block, `:63`), so one failure hides the tests after it. Round 4 recorded exactly that: five tests "did not run". Rounds 6 and 8 recorded two more each, behind the Text-app fit failure at `tests/e2e/07-apps-player.spec.ts:347` (`docs/reviews/2026-11-20-stage-c-roles-round-6/grok-runtime.md:110`, `docs/reviews/2026-11-21-email-alerts-round-8/grok-runtime.md:36`).

**Proposal.** Two small pieces, both green today.

```js
// eslint.config.js, test files and tests/e2e
{ selector: ":matches(MemberExpression[object.name=/^(it|test|describe)$/], MemberExpression[object.property.name='describe']):matches([property.name=/^(skip|skipIf|runIf|todo|fixme|only|fails|fail)$/])",
  message: 'A skipped, focused or expected-to-fail test is a red run (U-15): it hides a failure, or every other test. Fix the test or delete it in this change; there is no allow-list for a declared skip.' }
```

As built in step 2 (`NO_SKIPPED_TESTS` in `eslint.config.js`), the selector also covers `suite`, the chained forms (`concurrent`, `sequential`, `serial`, `parallel`, `step`) and the options form. A declared skip has no allow-list: the lint cannot read `tests/skip-allowlist.json`, and pointing authors at it would lead to disable comments, so the message says fix or delete.

`scripts/check-test-results.ts` (rules in `scripts/lib/test-results-check.ts`, unit-tested like `scripts/lib/audit-check.ts`) runs after Playwright and keeps the first exit code:

- fail on any test whose status is skipped, did-not-run or flaky, unless it is in `tests/skip-allowlist.json` with an owner, a reason and a `reviewBy` date no more than 90 days out (the shape and window of `scripts/audit-exceptions.json`). The allow-list governs these runtime results only; a declared skip has none (step 2's lint). This gate is also the layer that sees a runtime skip (`ctx.skip()`, `testInfo.skip()`), which no lint can;
- fail when the total is below the number committed in `tests/test-counts.json`, so a `grep` or `testMatch` typo cannot shrink the run;
- upload the result file always, not only on failure.

The same checker reads a Vitest JSON report per project and requires zero pending and todo tests (**unverified**: the field names in Vitest 5.1).

**Other ways a red state looks green today**, each with its fix:

| Where | Problem | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `.github/workflows/ci.yml:96` | a skipped job shows as neutral | section 4.6 |
| `tests/playwright.layout.config.ts` | two browser checks that need no service are run by no script and no CI step | add `npx playwright test -c tests/playwright.layout.config.ts` to the quality job; a unit test that every Playwright config is run by CI |
| `tests/e2e/11-accessibility.spec.ts:85` | a `networkidle` timeout is swallowed, so a page that never settles is scanned anyway | assert a settled state |
| `tests/e2e/03-isolation.spec.ts:48-53`, `tests/e2e/14-roles.spec.ts:320` | absence asserted without checking the request succeeded | `expectListedWithout(api, path, ids)`: status 200 and parsed items first |
| `tests/e2e/global-teardown.ts:47-51`, `tests/e2e/14-roles.spec.ts:199-206` | a failed sign-out, or a failed restore of the seeded roles in the shared e2e database, only warns | record it and fail the results check |
| `apps/api/test/player.integration.test.ts:142` | shuffle with `Math.random()`: a failing order cannot be replayed | seeded shuffle (step 2: a fixed scrambled order) |
| every suite | no floor on the number of tests | `tests/test-counts.json` |
| `vitest.config.ts:21-51` | a test file outside the include globs never runs (no orphan today) | `scripts/lib/test-inventory.test.ts`: every test file matches exactly one project (built in step 2) |

### 4.2 Fault injection for a lost COMMIT and for concurrency

**Faults today.** `losingCommitAnswers` (`apps/api/test/support.ts:758-789`) is used by two upload tests only (`apps/api/test/upload.integration.test.ts:692`, `:740`). `pg_terminate_backend` mid-transaction appears twice: `packages/db/test/rls.integration.test.ts:773-795`, and the alert run's lock connection in `tests/integration/display-alerts.test.ts:373-452`. `whileAuditFails` covers five audit actions (`tests/integration/credential-audit.test.ts:43-61`). One test injects a database refusal through its own pool proxy (`apps/api/test/members.integration.test.ts:497-540`, a `23001` on the role update).

**Concurrency today.** Covered: pairing claims (`apps/api/test/pairing.integration.test.ts:45`; `packages/db/test/sec.integration.test.ts:424`, `:445`, `:802`, `:823`), publish and push with one key (`apps/api/test/publish.integration.test.ts:73`; `apps/api/test/push.integration.test.ts:773`, `:1212`), a held-lock interleaving (`push.integration.test.ts:1316`), delete against assign (`apps/api/test/playlists.integration.test.ts:627`), heartbeats, and the upload quota. Added with stage C and the alerts: a role lowered, a member removed or signed out everywhere during a push or an upload (`apps/api/test/roles.integration.test.ts:311`, `:338`, `:362`, `:377`; `apps/api/test/upload.integration.test.ts:912`), a revocation against a sign-in (`tests/integration/reauth.test.ts:307`), the incident script against a held member row (`tests/integration/session-revoke-all.test.ts:192`), two demotions of the last Owners at once (`packages/db/test/migration-0013.integration.test.ts:539`), and two alert runs at once (`tests/integration/display-alerts.test.ts:318`). **Not covered:** revoke against claim, against heartbeat and against revoke; two saves of one playlist, schedule or layout (step 41c adds them with the version check of D24); a single-session sign-out against a request in flight; credential lookup against revoke (step 32 adds it, asserting the stated window of D8).

**Harness.**

```ts
// apps/api/test/support/faults.ts
faultyPool(pool, { at: /INSERT INTO audit_event/, mode: 'lost-answer' | 'never-sent' | 'backend-killed' })
// apps/api/test/support/race.ts
holdAt(pool, /UPDATE player_registration/)      // -> { reached: Promise<void>, release(): void }
```

`holdAt` exists now as `statementHolds().hold` (`apps/api/test/support.ts:799-866`, the same `{ reached, release }` shape), used in `roles.integration.test.ts`, `upload.integration.test.ts` and `reauth.test.ts`. What is left: move it and `losingCommitAnswers` into `support/faults.ts` and `support/race.ts`, and add the `never-sent` and `backend-killed` modes, which today are written by hand in each test (`members.integration.test.ts:497-540`, `display-alerts.test.ts:405`).

- `apps/api/test/commit-outcome.integration.test.ts`, table-driven over the 35 state-changing operator routes in the shared inventory `tests/integration/routes.ts:14-77` (main moved the list there; `tests/integration/session-csrf.test.ts:42-63` checks it against the routes the app serves, and `tests/integration/roles-matrix.test.ts:19` reads it too), plus the player routes and the public alert-address confirm route (`apps/api/src/routes/alert-recipients.ts:176`). For each fault mode: the audit row exists if and only if the change exists; no stored object is deleted on an unknown outcome; a retry with the same idempotency key replays.
- `apps/api/test/races.integration.test.ts` for the missing pairs: exactly one winner, one audit row, never a second active registration for a display.

**"Manifest version" means the publication id (D10).** There is no per-display version counter: the publication id is the ETag (`packages/contracts/src/player.ts:15`), the player compares ids only (`apps/player-web/src/core/sync.ts:129`), and `schemaVersion` 1 to 5 is a format version, which belongs to CU-01 only. The invariant (rule U-14): a display's `desired_publication_id` changes only in a transaction that inserts a publication for that display, and it then equals a publication inserted in that transaction. `desired_at` is not used, because it comes from the application clock. The step 33 test snapshots every display's pointer before and after each route, a missing delivery row counting as null.

### 4.3 Migrate-forward tests from old snapshots

**Today.** Upgrade tests start from four levels: 0004 (`packages/db/test/migration-0005.integration.test.ts:336-354`, and `tests/integration/migration-backfill.test.ts:217-237` through the API), 0011 (`packages/db/test/migration-0012.integration.test.ts:162-206`), 0012 (`packages/db/test/migration-0013.integration.test.ts:229-257`) and 0013 (`packages/db/test/migration-0014.integration.test.ts:48-63`). Each new migration's test starts from the level just before it. Levels 0005 to 0010 are never a starting point. No dump or snapshot is stored and the repository has no tags. Two weak points: the 0011 fixture is seeded through today's TypeScript helpers (`migration-0012.integration.test.ts:174-203`), so a later signature change breaks an old-level fixture. That has happened once: when 0013 made `owner` the default role of `createOrg` (`packages/db/test/support.ts:138`), the 0011 fixture had to pass `{ role: 'operator' }` (`migration-0012.integration.test.ts:176-178`). The 0013 fixture uses the same helper (`migration-0014.integration.test.ts:59-60`); the 0004 and 0012 fixtures are raw SQL valid at their level (`migration-0005.integration.test.ts:69`, `migration-backfill.test.ts:75`, `migration-0013.integration.test.ts:135-181`). And five files each reset the shared `tinnox_test` database to an old level and must remember to restore it.

**Design: a generator, not dumps.**

- The released levels are derived from the directory, with no hand-kept list and no tags: 0004 and every later file in `db/migrations` (D13). A branch's own new file is a level too, so its seeder lands with it.
- `packages/db/test/levels/seed-NNNN.ts` seeds each level in raw SQL that is valid at that level. A level with no seeder fails the test; a no-op seeder states its reason. In `packages/db/test/levels/**`, imports of `@tinnox/*` and relative imports that leave the folder are banned.
- `atLevel(level)` in `packages/db/test/levels.ts` replays to that level in a dedicated `tinnox_migrate_test` database, created the way `tests/e2e/support/database.ts:54-74` creates its own. No caller can then leave the shared database behind.
- One `packages/db/test/migrate-forward.integration.test.ts` runs one chain (D13): replay to 0004 and seed, apply and seed each level in turn, then upgrade to `HEAD` with the real runner and assert: the catalogue snapshot (reuse `schemaSnapshot`, `migration-0005.integration.test.ts:211-250`) equals a fresh `HEAD` build; row counts are kept; every stored manifest parses with the current contracts schema; audit rows are unchanged; a second run applies nothing; the catalogue test of section 3.6 passes.

This is the check that could have caught what 0010 missed and 0012 repaired. Cost: an estimated few seconds per level (**not measured**).

### 4.4 Property-based tests with fast-check

`fast-check` is not installed. Schemas are zod. `packages/domain/src` exports 35 pure functions, tested by fixed examples; six of them are the role rules of stage C (`packages/domain/src/permissions.ts`) and one is the offline-delay rule of the e-mail alerts (`offlineDue`).

Generators have to be written by hand, because the constraints that matter sit in `superRefine` checks (`checkLayoutZones`, `checkScheduleRule`, the manifest body checks), which a schema-to-generator tool cannot see. They live in `*.test-support.ts` files, which the domain purity lint already exempts (`eslint.config.js:66`); tests sit beside the code as `*.property.test.ts`, which the unit project already includes (`vitest.config.ts:22`). Each generator carries a self-check: its output parses with the schema.

| # | Property | Function under test | Generator |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | a rule covers exactly `ticked days × length` minutes a week | `eventCovers` (`packages/contracts/src/schedule.ts:58`) | rules accepted by the schema |
| 2 | at any instant exactly one playlist slice is on air, equal to a ten-line oracle | `playableIndexes` (`apps/player-web/src/core/time-rules.ts:69`), which reads the clock through `localWallClock` (`packages/contracts/src/schedule.ts:112`), shared with the alert check | version-4 manifests; instants 2026 to 2035; every IANA zone the runtime supports (today's examples cover three zones) |
| 3 | an accepted schedule plan builds, parses, and is version 4 exactly when an event names a non-default playlist | `planSchedulePublication` (`packages/domain/src/schedule.ts:31`) | 1 to 8 playlists, 0 to 50 events |
| 4 | zone rectangles are non-negative and inside the area; zones the schema accepts never overlap in pixels; a shared edge stays shared | `zoneRect` (`packages/domain/src/geometry.ts:68`) | canvases within the schema; guillotine splits into 2 to 4 boxes |
| 5 | an accepted layout plan builds a version-5 manifest | `planLayoutPublication` (`packages/domain/src/layout.ts:25`) | as 4, **plus a database-shaped generator**: 1 to 4 boxes, overlap allowed |
| 6 | contain stays inside, cover fills, aspect kept | `fitRect` (`geometry.ts:81`) | sides 1 to 16,384 |
| 7 | the version chosen equals the documented maximum over the whole feature lattice | `manifestVersionFor` (`packages/domain/src/manifest.ts:49`) | exhaustive, no randomness |
| 8 | a draft matches the manifest built from it after a JSON round trip, and stops matching after any one player-visible change | `draftMatchesManifest`, `buildManifest` | drafts from 3 and 5 |
| 9 | any arrival order of heartbeats ends at the report with the highest sequence | `reduceHeartbeat` (`packages/domain/src/delivery-state.ts:70`) | permutations |
| 10 | any changed claim of a signed media URL fails verification | `signMediaUrl`, `verifyMediaUrl` (`apps/api/src/security.ts:63`, `:83`) | field mutations |
| 11 | the offline delay comes due exactly when a minute-by-minute count of working minutes since the report reaches it (14-day rule included); never before the delay has elapsed; once due, stays due as `now` grows; null hours or an unknown zone count every minute | `offlineDue` (`packages/domain/src/delivery-state.ts:161`) | delays from `AlertOfflineMinutesSchema` (`packages/contracts/src/display.ts:60`); working hours the schema accepts; instants 2026 to 2035; every IANA zone the runtime supports (today's examples cover one zone, `delivery-state.test.ts:188`) |
| 12 | `endSessionUrl` with tokens of 0 to 8,192 characters always parses with `LogoutResponseSchema` (UTX-01) | `endSessionUrl` (`apps/api/src/auth/oidc.ts:194-200`) | token lengths and characters |
| 13 | for any sequence of read outcomes, a timer is pending whenever the poll function would answer non-null (UTX-03) | `useLoad`'s scheduler (`apps/portal/src/hooks/useLoad.ts:105-114`) | outcome sequences under fake timers |
| 14 | the player restores its active publication for any manifest `schemaVersion` and any evicted subset (FCX-02) | the re-check in `apps/player-web/src/core/sync.ts` | manifests 1 to 5 and subsets |

**Suspected to fail today.** From a seeded in-memory run (not fast-check, so evidence and not proof):

- Property 5 with the database-shaped generator fails: overlapping zones, or a single zone, plan `ok` and then `buildManifest` throws. This is LYX-03, still open.
- "Accepted implies builds" fails for an unknown time zone at `schemaVersion` 2 or later and passes at 1 (`packages/domain/src/manifest.ts:100-101`, a recorded shortcut).
- It fails for a media window whose year is outside 1000 to 9999: the column is `timestamp(0)` with an order check only (`db/migrations/0006_stage_b_slice1.sql:25-31`). The domain half was reproduced; whether the runtime role can store such a value is **unverified**. No review mentions it.
- With schema-valid input, nothing failed in 20,000 layouts and 4,000 schedules.
- Property 11 held in 5,000 seeded cases against the oracle above, over all 417 zones of the Node runtime used, with whole-minute times and quarter-hour working hours. Times with seconds were not tried.

The role matrix needs no property: it is a finite table and is already tested exhaustively, all 64 role and key pairs and all 16 `canManage` pairs (`packages/domain/src/permissions.test.ts:77`, `:115`), and every operator route under every role against an oracle written from DEC-35 (`tests/integration/roles-matrix.test.ts`).

**Twins.** `packages/db/test/twins.integration.test.ts`: for generated schedule rules, layout zone sets, media windows and addresses, the contract's parsed output is compared with whether "an `INSERT` as `tinnox_app` succeeds", inside a rolled-back transaction (the contracts trim and lower-case, `packages/contracts/src/members.ts:30`, `packages/contracts/src/alerts.ts:33`, so the raw input would report false mismatches). It would flag `layout_zone` today (the database accepts overlap and a single zone; the contract does not) and probably the media window year. Stage C and the alerts add twins of their own. The offline delay matches its check exactly (`packages/contracts/src/display.ts:60-64`, `db/migrations/0014_display_alerts.sql:36-39`). The address checks are looser than the contracts: `alert_recipient_email_shape` and `member_invite_email_shape` (`0014_display_alerts.sql:77-79`, `0013_roles_and_members.sql:204`) accept `a@b` and `a@b@c`, which `InviteEmailSchema` refuses (`packages/contracts/src/members.ts:33`). So both would be flagged too. Decided by shape (D7): row-local twins get a database guard in one new migration with the next free number (the two address checks, re-added with the contract's own shape and no ASCII restriction, and a year range on the media window); cross-row twins (zone overlap, zone count) stay in code, held by the planner and property 5. The allow-list holds only cross-row entries and entries where the database is looser on character class.

Seed policy (D11): `fast-check` is pinned exactly in the root `devDependencies`, without the Vitest wrapper. Every run uses a random seed, locally and in CI, and the failure report prints the seed and the shrunk counterexample. Each counterexample is committed with its fix as a plain example test in the same file; no seed or path is ever committed, which a lint on the options of `fc.assert`, `fc.check` and `fc.configureGlobal` holds.

### 4.5 axe accessibility checks

**Today.** `expectAccessible` (`tests/e2e/support/a11y.ts:10-36`) runs the WCAG A and AA tags with no disabled rule and no excluded selector, and fails on `serious` or `critical` only; `incomplete` results are dropped. `tests/e2e/11-accessibility.spec.ts` scans 19 hand-listed pages (`:89-109`, now with Users and alert addresses) in light and dark, plus the schedule editor with errors, four dialogs (create schedule, push, add alert address with an error, invite), four screen tabs, one menu and the public alert confirm page in its "not valid" state: 46 tests, all as the seeded Owner. `axe-core` 4.14.0 is installed. It runs only in the manual e2e job; the component project (which gained a setup file, `apps/portal/src/componentTests.setup.ts`) has no axe at all.

**Not covered.** Six routes (`apps/portal/src/App.tsx:120-124`, `:187`: five media sub-pages, not-found); the page `RequirePermission` shows to a role that may not open a route (`apps/portal/src/shell/ForbiddenPage.tsx`); the sign-in, session-error and checking screens (`:196-201`); the route error page (`:114`); the alert confirm page's other states; every dialog but four; any role but Owner; phone widths; and all seven player screens (`apps/player-web/src/ui/screens.ts:33-116`). And the sweep cannot see finding FCU-05 at all: `region` is a best-practice rule, outside the WCAG tags.

**Proposal.**

1. A JSX-free route manifest, `apps/portal/src/routes.manifest.ts`, that `App.tsx` builds its router from. The spec's fixtures are typed `satisfies Record<PagePath, ...>`, so **a route without a scan fails `npm run typecheck`**. Precedent: `tests/integration/routes.ts:14-77`, checked by `tests/integration/session-csrf.test.ts:42-63`. Main already exports the route table (`apps/portal/src/App.tsx:110`) and `apps/portal/src/shell/RequirePermission.test.tsx:36-47` reads its guarded routes from it, but by hand-typed paths; the typed path list is what is missing.
2. Add `region`, `landmark-one-main` and `aria-dialog-name` as blocking rules. **Unverified** whether they pass on today's pages; the pull request may surface findings.
3. An axe helper in the Vitest component project, which runs Chromium on every push (`vitest.config.ts:31-41`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:43`), covering the shell screens outside the route table.
4. Player screens scanned under the stackless Playwright config.
5. A dated allow-list of rule ids instead of the blanket `serious`/`critical` threshold.
6. Run the route-manifest scan once more as the seeded Viewer, and fail on a list of instruction words (Add, Push, Delete, Edit) in visible text (SCR-18 to SCR-20).

### 4.6 e2e with Keycloak in CI

**Today.** The e2e job runs only on manual dispatch or when the `RUN_E2E` variable is true (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:96`); the comment gives slowness as the reason (`:87-93`). No `RUN_E2E` repository variable is set, so the job is skipped on every push. It was dispatched by hand twice on 21 November on the PR 34 branch: one run cancelled, one passed, in about 20 minutes on `ubuntu-24.04`. The integration job **already starts Keycloak** with the realm import on every push (`:66-67`), and since `infra:up` gained it, Mailpit too (`package.json:18`), though no integration test calls either (they use a stub provider, `apps/api/test/support.ts:65-73`, a recording mailer, `:83-94`, and a fake identity-provider reader for provisioning, `tests/integration/provision-member.test.ts:1-3`). So Keycloak's and Mailpit's start-up is paid for already.

**What stands in the way.** No secret: every credential is generated per run. The obstacles are run time (about 6 minutes at 25 tests and 12.7 minutes at 66 are recorded, then 15.8 minutes at 143, `docs/reviews/2026-11-20-stage-c-roles-round-6/grok-runtime.md:108`, and 17.0 minutes at 150, `docs/reviews/2026-11-21-email-alerts-round-8/grok-runtime.md:36`; in CI it is now known, about 20 minutes at 150 tests; the job limit is 40 minutes), one recorded flake with no cause found (the offline-reload step, `docs/reviews/2026-11-17-security-round-3/README.md:189-190`), a host-dependent failure (the Text-app fit check failed in both of those full runs, put down to Linux font metrics, `tests/e2e/07-apps-player.spec.ts:347`; CMS-21 changed the check, checked on macOS with Linux-like font metrics, `docs/reviews/2026-11-21-email-alerts-round-8/README.md:85`, and it has since passed in the Linux end-to-end job, `STATUS.md:173`), a single worker and one API port, a PostgreSQL container restart inside `tests/e2e/01-journey.spec.ts:641`, the alerts spec waiting on the API's 60-second alert loop (`apps/api/src/main.ts:19`; up to 150 seconds per e-mail, `tests/e2e/15-alerts.spec.ts:50`), and one-time codes that each sign-in must wait up to 30 seconds for, now across five Keycloak users (`scripts/setup-dev.mjs:168`). The full suite has now run on Node 22 (the major version `.nvmrc` names) in the round 6 and round 8 reviews, round 6 on a Linux VM (`docs/reviews/2026-11-20-stage-c-roles-round-6/grok-runtime.md:5`, `:110`); it has run in CI once, dispatched by hand for PR 34 (`STATUS.md:151`).

**Staged path.**

0. **Now, nearly free:** add `npm run verify:oidc` to the integration job (Keycloak is already up; the script now also checks the forced re-sign-in and the read-only provisioning client, `scripts/verify-oidc-login.mjs:213`, `:342`), and the stackless Playwright config to the quality job (Chromium is already installed at `ci.yml:42`).
1. `.github/workflows/e2e.yml`: nightly on `main` as a three-copy matrix, and on every pull request as one job with a fixed check name, no path filter and no `if:`; `workflow_dispatch` stays. The manual e2e job leaves `ci.yml` (`:94-96`) in the same pull request (D14). Always upload the result file, run the results check of section 4.1, and write counts and duration to the job summary.
2. e2e becomes a required check, not strict, when steps 3 and 36 are merged, the 20 latest scheduled runs on `main` all succeeded as three-copy matrices (a failure or a cancellation restarts the count), the slowest job took at most 30 minutes, and the pull-request job has one fixed check name, no path filter and no `if:`. It is demoted after two failures on `main` within 14 days that pass on re-run (D14). To shard later: move the container-restart test to a nightly-only project and generate one set of the five Keycloak users per shard (`operator`, `secondOrgOperator`, `admin`, `editor`, `viewer`, `scripts/setup-dev.mjs:147-170`). Mailpit can stay shared: the alerts spec finds its mail by a per-run address (`tests/e2e/15-alerts.spec.ts:22-24`).

**Documents.** e2e counts and durations are typed by hand in at least five documents and already disagree (`README.md:84` says about 6.5 minutes, `ci.yml:90` seven, the last full run 17.0; `docs/dev-handoff.md:23` says 25 tests, `tests/README.md:61` says 143, and the suite now lists 150). Replace them with a link to the run. Until e2e is a required check (staged item 2 above, step 37 in section 6), a control proven only by an e2e test is not proven by CI, and section 5.2 says so mechanically.

### 4.7 Is TLA+ worth it for pairing, revocation and manifest version?

**The protocol as built.**

| Transition | Code | Atomic? |
|---|---|---|
| create a pairing session | `apps/api/src/routes/player.ts:205-226` | one transaction with its audit row |
| claim: cancels older claims, revokes the active registration | `sec.pairing_claim` (`db/migrations/0013_roles_and_members.sql:415-495`, which replaced `0010_pairing_hardening.sql:29-108` and adds a removed-member refusal), `apps/api/src/routes/displays.ts:600-674` | one transaction; the member row re-read `FOR SHARE` first (`withOrgDecisionAllowed`, `apps/api/src/auth/permissions.ts:474`), then the operator's advisory lock, then the display's |
| poll, then complete | `routes/player.ts:270-352`; `sec.pairing_complete` (`0013_roles_and_members.sql:501-554`) | two transactions in one request |
| operator revoke, with cancel | `routes/displays.ts:677-718` | one transaction; member row re-read first (`withOrgAllowed`, `auth/permissions.ts:454`) |
| member removal, demotion or sign-out everywhere sets the claimer's cut-off; a later collection refuses an older claim (RCX-01) | `sec.session_revoke_membership` (`0013_roles_and_members.sql:570-598`) from `apps/api/src/routes/members.ts:349`, `:410`, `:449`; the check in `sec.pairing_complete` (`0013_roles_and_members.sql:527-535`) | each one transaction; collection locks the pairing row, then the member row `FOR SHARE`; a member change never locks pairing rows |
| player reset | `routes/player.ts:374-395` | one transaction |
| credential lookup, then the work | `routes/player.ts:153-187` | **lookup is autocommit; the work runs in a later transaction** |
| publish, move the desired pointer | `apps/api/src/publishing.ts:219-257`, `:798-844` | one transaction; key lock and display row lock |
| stage, re-check, activate (player) | `apps/player-web/src/core/staging.ts:153-304` | activation is one IndexedDB transaction |

| Invariant | Test today | Finding that broke it |
|---|---|---|
| a pairing code yields one device token | `sec.integration.test.ts:445`; `pairing.integration.test.ts:45` | none |
| at most one active registration per display (unique index, `db/migrations/0001_schema.sql:127`) | `sec.integration.test.ts:642`, `:764` | none |
| a revoke or a newer claim ends older claims | `sec.integration.test.ts:670`, `:705`, `:802` | SCX-01 |
| a claim by a removed member, or made at or before the claimer's cut-off, never completes, even after the member is brought back | `packages/db/test/migration-0013.integration.test.ts:919`, `:938`, `:946`; `tests/integration/provision-member.test.ts:521` | RCX-01 (round 6), which also closed SCR-04 |
| a revoked credential gets 401, old signed URLs too | `tests/integration/pairing-and-credentials.test.ts:220` | none; the in-flight window is stated (D8) and untested until step 32 |
| the desired pointer only moves to a new publication | `push.integration.test.ts:1212` | none; single writer, no constraint; step 33 adds the pointer snapshot test (D10) |
| liveness: a paired player converges to the desired publication | `tests/e2e/01-journey.spec.ts:400`, `:486`, `:558` | FCX-01, FCX-02, FCX-06, LYX-02 |

**Assessment: not now.** A model would be small (two players, two operators, three sessions, one display). But of the findings in this protocol, only SCX-01 and, in stage C, RCX-01 are design-level ordering bugs that a model at the natural abstraction would have caught, and each trace is four sequential steps with no concurrency (claim P1, claim P2, complete P2, complete P1; claim, remove the claimer, bring them back, complete). The first fix proposed for RCX-01, expiring the claims inside the removal, deadlocked with `sec.pairing_complete` (reproduced, `docs/reviews/2026-11-20-stage-c-roles-round-6/README.md:88`); lock order sits below that abstraction. SCX-05 was a race inside one SQL function, hidden as soon as the claim is modelled as atomic. SCX-04, SCU-01 and CX-01 were audit atomicity. SCX-03, FCX-01 and FCX-06 were implementation. The logic lives in PL/pgSQL spread over four migrations (0002, 0010, 0012, 0013), and nothing would tie a specification to it: it would be prose with a checker, which is the thing this plan is trying to have less of.

**Do these instead**, in this order:

1. A fast-check model-based test, `packages/db/test/pairing-model.integration.test.ts`: commands `Create`, `Claim`, `Poll`, `Complete`, `Revoke`, `Reset`, `Expire`, and since stage C `RemoveMember`, `Reinstate` and `EndSessions`, against the real `sec.*` functions, compared with a small TypeScript model. It reuses the helpers at `sec.integration.test.ts:66-94` and `packages/db/test/migration-0013.integration.test.ts:846`. **Acceptance: with migration 0010 removed, it finds the SCX-01 trace; with the cut-off condition taken out of `sec.pairing_complete`, it finds the RCX-01 trace.**
2. Two-transaction tests at statement granularity on the existing `twoTransactions` and `stillWaiting`, which moved to `packages/db/test/support.ts:308-338`: claim against complete, revoke against complete, removal against complete, lookup against revoke.
3. A state chart in `docs/player-protocol-v1.md`, the player half generated from the transition table (`apps/player-web/src/core/machine.ts:56`) so it cannot drift. The document does not yet name the claimer's removal or cut-off: the `consumed` row (`docs/player-protocol-v1.md:77`) says only that the claim "was cancelled".

**A second candidate for a model-based test: the alert episode (DEC-37).** Per display it has two states, no row or an open `display_alert` row (`db/migrations/0014_display_alerts.sql:45-53`), and its transitions are claimed by compare-and-set statements against heartbeats that arrive at any time (`apps/api/src/alerts.ts:224-256`). Commands `Heartbeat`, `Tick`, `Revoke`, `Repair`, `AlertsOff`, `BackOnlineOff` and two runners at once, against `runDisplayAlerts` with a recording mailer, would check that each episode sends at most one offline e-mail per address, and a reporting-again e-mail only after that one. Today `tests/integration/display-alerts.test.ts` checks this with 13 fixed cases, two runs at once (`:318`) and a lost lock (`:373`) among them. The role matrix is not a candidate: it is finite and already tested exhaustively (section 4.4).

**I would revisit TLA+ if** the protocol gains a second source of truth (offline pairing, several players per display, roll-back to an earlier publication, a push channel, a second delivery route with its own credentials), or if the model-based test keeps finding ordering bugs.

**The revocation window, stated (D8).** The device credential is looked up on the pool (`routes/player.ts:170`), and the manifest read, the URL issue and the heartbeat write run in a later transaction (`:404`, `:446`, `:584`). A request already past its lookup can complete after a revocation has committed. Stage C and the alerts left this path as it was: `sec.device_by_token_hash` is still the 0002 function, and the in-write role check covers operator sessions only. Decided: state the window, add no lock. Revocation applies to every request whose credential or signed-URL lookup starts after the revocation commits; a request already past the lookup finishes its one transaction (no overall limit: up to 5 s for a connection, then 15 s per statement), and a media response already started may run to the request timeout. Device writes may finish because they are untrusted telemetry that authorises no operator action (control B14). One side effect is closed: the heartbeat's write takes effect only while its registration is still active, so a late heartbeat from a replaced player cannot hide the new player's report. Step 32 tests both.

### 4.8 What the later review rounds add

Rounds 5 to 8 mostly repeated the gaps above. They also showed five that the list does not cover.

| Gap | Evidence | Proposal |
|---|---|---|
| The API never checks what it sends against the contract the portal parses it with | UTX-01: a sign-out URL longer than the schema allows failed only in the browser. The portal parses every answer (`apps/portal/src/api/client.ts:338`); the API validates none | In the integration harness only, an `onSend` hook parses every JSON answer with `ROUTE_RESPONSE_SCHEMAS` (section 2.5) and fails the test on a mismatch. **Unverified**: how many routes have a response schema today |
| A test that passes with its safeguard removed | UTX-02, RCX-03, RCU-03, EAX-11. Round 6 found its own role matrix counted a 500 as "allowed". Each fix there was proved by a mutation run done by hand | For each control tagged in section 5.2, record the mutation that must turn its test red, starting with the ones the reviews already ran (`tests/integration/roles-matrix.test.ts:81`, `packages/db/test/migration-0013.integration.test.ts:919`). The range-status lint of section 3.2 removes the commonest cause |
| Component tests do not render the way the app does | EAX-11: the app runs under `StrictMode` (`apps/portal/src/main.tsx:9`); all component test files but one do not | `configure({ reactStrictMode: true })` in `apps/portal/src/componentTests.setup.ts`. **Unverified** whether the component tests stay green |
| A check that depends on the host it runs on | EAU-03: a text-fit check failed on Linux font metrics; the fix (CMS-21) has since passed in the Linux end-to-end job (`STATUS.md:173`). EAX-09: the e2e API inherited the developer's mail settings | The e2e environment allow-list (section 2.5). Fonts stay a host property until e2e runs on the CI image on every change (the staged path of section 4.6) |
| The alert episode is a second small protocol | EAX-01, EAX-04, EAX-05, EAX-06 | The alert-episode model named in section 4.7, and a held-statement interleaving for the claim (`statementHolds`, section 4.2) |

## 5. Layer 4: prose

### 5.1 A slimmed `AGENTS.md`

Of the 58 atomic instructions in today's file (the AG rows of sections 1.1 to 1.6, counted clause by clause, so each stage C sentence at `AGENTS.md:16`, `:17` and `:23` counts on its own): 9 are enforced enough today (Target "done"), 8 stay prose (Target "keep"), 8 get a check but keep a judgment half (a layer number with "keep"), and the other 33 have a deterministic proposal with nothing left to judge. The slimmed file keeps the judgment halves and the reporting lines and points at the checks for the rest.

It should not be landed in one step. **Each pull request in section 6 that lands a check deletes the sentence that check replaces**, in both files, so the prose never claims more than the code enforces. The text below is where that ends up. Most check names below (the catalogue test, the scope types, `changeOrg`, `settle()`, the `check:*` scripts, `controls.json`, `upgrade-tests.json`) do not exist until their step in section 6 lands.

```markdown
# AGENTS.md

(orientation paragraph, unchanged)

## Code Review Rules

`.cursor/BUGBOT.md` is generated from this section; `npm run check:docs` fails when they differ. Edit this file only.

Report findings as Critical, High, Medium, Low or Observation. Cite `path:line` for every finding. Say what you could not verify by reading alone and mark it "unverified pending runtime". Check `docs/decisions-2026-11.md`, `docs/security/control-matrix.md`, the `residuals` of `docs/security/controls.json` and earlier rounds under `docs/reviews/` first: a known, documented residual is reported as such, not as a new finding.

A rule that a check enforces is not repeated here. When a check is red, fix the code, not the check. When you correct something a check could have caught, add the check in the same change, not a line here.

### 1. What the checks hold

- Roles, grants and ownership: `packages/db/test/catalogue.integration.test.ts`, which also fails when a `sec.*` function reads `member_invite`. A grant change, column grants included, shows as a diff of `packages/db/test/catalogue/grants.json`. `connectMigrator` in `packages/db`, used by the runner, the test reset, the seed and provisioning, refuses any role but `tinnox_migrator` (step 5a); at run time `createPool` and `assertRuntimeRole` refuse any role but `tinnox_app` (step 12); the API reads its configuration from an allow-list, and in every mode `loadConfig` refuses the migrator, superuser, Keycloak-admin and provisioning names (step 12), and lint bans them in `apps/*/src` and `packages/*/src`.
- Tenant scope: the `OrgId`, `OrgTx` and `PreOrgTx` types in `packages/db/src/tx.ts`; forced RLS on every table; the route enumeration in `tests/integration/cross-org.test.ts`, which compares the answer to another organisation's id with the answer to a never-issued id; the contracts unit test that no request schema or route pattern carries an organisation key. Role refusals: the start-up check over `ROUTE_PERMISSIONS` (`apps/api/src/auth/permissions.ts`) and `tests/integration/roles-matrix.test.ts` (every route, all four roles, the same 403 for an unknown and an own id).
- Audit: `changeOrg` writes the audit rows on the transaction of the change; `unaudited(reason)` is the only way to skip one.
- Errors: status comes from the error code; `settle()` for outcomes; no SQLSTATE or 403 in route files (`eslint.config.js`).
- Migrations: `packages/db/src/migrate.ts` and `npm run check:migrations`.
- Frontend: `eslint.config.js`; the axe route table in `tests/e2e/11-accessibility.spec.ts`.
- Documents: `npm run check:docs`, `npm run check:controls`.

### 2. Judgment calls

- Grants and secrets: is a new or wider grant to `tinnox_app` more than the feature needs? Can a change let the migrator's credentials or the provisioning reader secret reach the API, the media worker or the player in a way the checks do not see, for example a new process started with the root `.env`?
- Hosts: the migration runner, the seed and the provisioning script connect as `tinnox_migrator` only from a controlled host, and the database administrator runs the incident scripts. This is an operational rule no check can see.
- 403s: another organisation's id gets exactly the answer a never-issued id gets, and every 403 is decided without looking up the requested id. The one exception is the people routes' rank, self and grant rules (`docs/portal-v2/stage-c.md` section 1.6), and another organisation's id still answers 404 there. Is a new 403 decided before any lookup? Is a new after-lookup refusal one of the people-route rules?
- `sec.*`: does a new or changed function take only what it needs and return the minimum? A new cross-organisation read is High until justified; its reason goes in the register in the catalogue test.
- Audit: three things write a row: every successful change to tenant or security state; every attempt, whatever its result, at sign-in, pairing, revocation, upload and publishing; and authentication and authorisation refusals, thresholded. Other business-rule refusals, heartbeats, purges, session activity and login state write none. Two named exceptions record a refusal where nothing changed outside a change's transaction: `auditStandalone` (sign-in and device-credential refusals) and the scoped refusal-only transaction (SCR-32). Is a new kind of event security-relevant? Does a new kind of refusal belong in `auditStandalone`'s union? Is each `unaudited(reason)` true? Could `detail` carry content, a secret, a token, a signed URL or a query string?
- New kinds of tenant data: can a signed URL or device token for one organisation reach another's objects?
- Migrations: a dependence on data only present in development; a rewrite or long `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock on a large table; whether the reason given in `db/upgrade-tests.json` for having no upgrade test is good.
- A new `// shortcut:` comment, allow-list entry, exception file entry or residual: is the limit acceptable at all, who may accept it, and does it have an owner and a date?

### 3. Skills for each kind of round

- Frontend rounds (portal, player, `packages/app-render`): also apply `.agents/skills/web-design-guidelines/`.
- Database rounds (migrations, RLS, `packages/db`): also apply `.agents/skills/postgres-best-practices/`. Its examples use Supabase's `authenticated` and `service_role` roles and `auth.uid()`. In this repo those map to `tinnox_app` with `app.org_id`, and there is no service role: nothing at runtime may bypass RLS except the `sec.*` functions. Ignore advice that only applies to Supabase's hosted platform.
```

Three notes:

- **Generate, do not share (D15).** `.cursor/BUGBOT.md` is generated from the `## Code Review Rules` section and checked. There is no path-scoped or override rule file anywhere: Codex reads an `AGENTS.override.md` in place of `AGENTS.md`, and nested files load inconsistently in both tools (Codex by working directory, Bugbot by the changed paths). `AGENTS.md` stays under 16 KiB, because Codex's 32 KiB limit also counts a user's global file. Step 1's `check:docs` holds all three.
- **The Supabase mapping** (`AGENTS.md:43`) stays inline, as the draft above shows (D15): it states the role model, and whether either reviewer loads `.agents/skills/` is unverified.
- **Do not add rules about agents writing comments or markdown.** The talk bans most comments; this repository's evidence does not support that (section 3.2). What it supports is the shortcut ledger below.

### 5.2 Linking control-matrix claims to the tests that prove them

**Today.** `docs/security/control-matrix.md` has 67 rows in five tables. Only the 17 rows of section 1 have ids (B1 to B17; B16, application roles, and B17, e-mail alerts, came with stage C). Evidence is prose: a status word, backticked files and quoted test titles, with the shorthand `e2e/...` for `tests/e2e/...` (`control-matrix.md:8`).

The references are in good order: every cited test file exists (three are cited by bare file name, `:22`, `:24`, `:36`) and all 85 quoted titles resolve to a test or `describe` title (eight are quoted as a prefix ending in "…"). What has drifted is counts and run-status prose: "26 tests" (`:24`) where `packages/db/test/rls.integration.test.ts` has 34; "7 tests" for `cross-org.test.ts` (`:24`) where it has 9; "every tenant table (22" (`:24`) where `TENANT_TABLES` lists 25; "in the 366" (`:74`) against 395 at `:48`. `docs/security/threat-model.md:122`, which says the CI workflow has never executed, sits in the dated section 8 (`:104`), so it is a frozen snapshot, not drift (D17).

By row: 15 are proven by a named test, 24 by a test or CI step the row does not name, 7 are prose only, 1 is stale, 20 are manual by nature (operations, policy, hosting). These are the earlier split moved for the three rows stage C added or changed (B16 and B17 name their tests; the ISM roles row at `:66` now says "TESTED as B1, B2 and B16"); the other 64 rows were not re-classified (**unverified**).

**The gap that matters is at suite level.** Three claims marked TESTED are proven only by the e2e job, which runs only when dispatched by hand, so CI does not prove them on every push:

- the `HttpOnly` and `SameSite` cookie attributes (B2): only `tests/e2e/01-journey.spec.ts:155-156`. No Vitest test asserts a `Set-Cookie` attribute (integration tests read the header only for the cookie's name and value, for example `tests/integration/session-csrf.test.ts:315`);
- password plus one-time code sign-in (B1): only `tests/e2e/01-journey.spec.ts:145` and `npm run verify:oidc` (`scripts/verify-oidc-login.mjs`, which since stage C also drives `prompt=login` and takes the role to sign in as), which no CI step runs. `tests/integration/reauth.test.ts` covers the forced re-sign-in with a stub provider, not the password and code;
- the portal's frame and sniffing headers (B9): only `tests/e2e/05-hardening.spec.ts:74`. The player's dev and preview headers have a unit test (`apps/player-web/src/build-checks.test.ts:19-25`); the portal's (`apps/portal/vite.config.ts:15-18`) have none.

**Mechanism: control ids in test titles, checked by a script.** The repository already has the habit: 29 test and `describe` titles carry finding ids such as "(round 3, SCX-01)".

1. Keep the B ids and split rows into atomic controls: `B2.1` cookie attributes, `B2.2` CSRF token, and so on (50 to 70 in all, **estimate**).
2. A small register carries what a script needs; the matrix stays hand-written prose (its cells are long judgment text, and generating it would be a poor trade):

   ```json
   // docs/security/controls.json
   { "controls": [
     { "id": "B2.2", "title": "CSRF token on every state change", "threats": ["T6"], "proof": "test" },
     { "id": "B10",  "proof": "ci-step", "step": "npm run scan:secrets" },
     { "id": "B2.1", "threats": ["T6"], "proof": "e2e-only", "owner": "...", "lastRun": "2026-11-18", "reviewBy": "2026-12-18" },
     { "id": "B15",  "proof": "manual", "owner": "...", "lastVerified": "2026-11-14", "reviewBy": "2027-02-12" },
     { "id": "B8.9", "proof": "none", "reason": "no malicious-content scan (ISM-2059)" } ],
     "residuals": [
       { "findings": ["UTX-04"], "reason": "...", "owner": "...", "acceptedBy": "...", "acceptedOn": "...",
         "source": "docs/reviews/2026-11-19-pr29-review/README.md:47", "reviewBy": "..." } ] }
   ```

   The `residuals` array is kept apart from `controls`, so a residual never counts as cover for a threat (D26). Each entry has its finding ids, a reason, an owner, who accepted it and when, the source path and line, and either a guard (a test title that carries the finding id) or a review date at most 90 days ahead. Rounds 1 to 8 and the stage C review are backfilled; a residual that touches a client decision goes to the decisions register instead.

3. Tests carry the id: `it('[B2.2] requires the CSRF token of this session on every state-changing request', ...)`.
4. `npm run check:controls` (`scripts/controls.ts`, rules in `scripts/lib/controls-check.ts`, unit tests beside it) runs in the quality job and needs no database. It fails with messages such as:
   - `control B5.3 is marked "test" but no test title carries [B5.3]: tag the test that proves it, or change its proof in docs/security/controls.json`
   - `tests/integration/x.test.ts:42 carries [B99], which is not in docs/security/controls.json`
   - `control B2.1 is proven only by tests/e2e/01-journey.spec.ts:145; the e2e job does not run on every push: add a unit or integration test, or mark it "e2e-only" with lastRun and reviewBy`
   - `manual control B15 passed its review date: verify again and update lastVerified`
   - `threat T9 has no control` (true today: no matrix row names T9)
   - `control-matrix.md:24 cites rls.integration.test.ts, which is not a repository path`
   - `residual UTX-04 has no guard test whose title carries UTX-04 and no review date within 90 days`
   - `docs/reviews/<round>/README.md:12: the outcome "Kept" must start with Fixed, Accepted, Refuted or No change` (rounds from the next one on)
   - `docs/reviews/<round>/README.md:14 accepts a finding that the residuals of docs/security/controls.json do not list`
5. Later, a second stage reads the Vitest and Playwright result files and fails when a tagged test did not pass. That catches run-time skips, which the static scan cannot.

**Manual controls.** A `lastVerified` date that CI checks fits where a verification act exists: `verify:oidc`, the action-pin check, the log search for secrets, a restore on the real backup system, the threat-model review. It does not fit rows that are "not done" or "needs TINNOX input": a date there invites a rubber stamp. Those get `proof: "none"` and an owner.

`npm run check:docs` (`scripts/check-docs.ts`, rules in `scripts/lib/docs-check.ts`, in the house style of `scripts/audit.ts`), one step in the quality job:

| Rule | Flags today |
|---|---|
| `AGENTS.md` and `.cursor/BUGBOT.md` match from `## Code Review Rules` | nothing |
| over `git ls-files` (a filesystem glob would also find the worktree copies under `.claude/worktrees/` of the main checkout, which only a local `.git/info/exclude` hides): no `AGENTS.override.md` anywhere, the root included, and no `AGENTS.md` or `.cursor/BUGBOT.md` but the root two (D15) | nothing |
| `AGENTS.md` at most 16 KiB and `.cursor/BUGBOT.md` at most 30,000 characters (D15) | nothing (6,306 and 5,861 bytes at `002c3d6`; 7,219 and 6,774 bytes on 2026-11-22) |
| every backticked repository path in `AGENTS.md`, `BUGBOT.md`, `README.md`, the decisions register and the three security documents exists (shorthand expanded; a bare document name is also tried under `docs/`; placeholders and gitignored paths skipped) | `README.md:200` (`scratchpad/`); the bare file names `migration-0013.integration.test.ts` at `control-matrix.md:22`, `rls.integration.test.ts` at `:24`, and `write-guard.test.ts` at `:36` and `threat-model.md:90`; all fixed in step 1, which also fixed a hit this reading missed, the DEC-38 row's `docs/security/controls.json` path |

| every action is pinned by a 40-character commit, every external image by digest. As built (step 1, review fix CHK-6), the rule reads workflows, compose files, Dockerfiles and every tracked script or source file that runs docker, and exempts an image a compose service builds here | `scripts/scan-secrets.mjs:17` (gitleaks by tag only); the new Mailpit image is pinned by digest (`infra/docker-compose.yml:72`). Step 1 pinned gitleaks by digest |
| dated sections are frozen snapshots: every file under `docs/reviews/**`, `docs/prototype-validation.md`, and any heading that ends in a date in brackets; the path and count rules skip them, and a snapshot is corrected only by a dated inline line or a later dated section (that a snapshot is not rewritten is a review rule: `check:docs` only finds dated sections). In live text (the documents above plus `docs/dev-handoff.md` and `tests/README.md`) a hand-typed count of tests, cases, routes, tables or `sec` functions may appear only in a sentence that carries its own date or run link, not with the date elsewhere in its cell or paragraph; a product limit ("at most 20 addresses") passes (D17) | `control-matrix.md:8` (TESTED defined by the run of 2026-11-14, which B16 and B17 postdate), `:24` (three counts), `:36`, `:37`, `:74`; `threat-model.md:90`; `docs/dev-handoff.md:23` (25 e2e tests; the suite lists 150 today), `:147` (17 `sec` functions; the test pins 21); `tests/README.md:61` (143 e2e tests; the suite lists 150) and `:63` (59 operator routes; 62 today, `tests/integration/roles-matrix.test.ts:118`). Step 1 fixes every hit the rule reports; this list is from reading, not a run. As run in step 1: `control-matrix.md:8` holds no count (it is a run-status claim) and `docs/dev-handoff.md:147` carries a date in its sentence, so neither is a hit; both were corrected by hand. Not in this list: three dated backup-run records in `docs/security/operations-and-release-checklist.md`, given snapshot headings |

**The shortcut ledger.** There are 61 `// shortcut:` markers in source (API 16, worker 1, player 14, portal 15, app-render 3, domain 10, contracts 1, scripts 1), each recording an accepted limit. They are the one place where this repository's comments work the way the talk warns about: the next author can copy one. A committed ledger (path, text hash, owner) and a check that every marker is in it, and every entry still has its marker, makes each new one a visible decision.

## 6. Ordered steps, one pull request each

Rules for every step: the check and the fixes it needs land in the same pull request, so `main` stays green. For a large refactor the guardrail lands first as a lint with an allow-list of today's files, and each later pull request removes its file from the list. Each step also deletes the `AGENTS.md` sentence it replaces (and regenerates `BUGBOT.md`). "Verify" always includes `npm run lint`, `typecheck`, `test` and `test:integration`; the column names what is specific. A step with a letter (12a, 23a) was added after review rounds 5 to 8 or by a decision of section 7 (5a, 6a, 41c); it sits where it should land, and the other numbers did not move. Steps 1, 2, 5, 5a, 7, 8, 9 and 10 were built on 2026-11-22 as one commit each on one branch, `refactor/poteto-pyramid-guardrails`, each reviewed on its own and the branch reviewed as a whole; they went up as one pull request, by Michael's decision of 22 November 2026 (section 7). Section 6.1 records them.

**A. Guardrails that are green today or need a handful of fixes**

| # | Adds | Surfaces today | Risk | Verify |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `npm run check:docs` (5.3), its rules over `git ls-files`: rule-file sync, paths (a bare document name is also tried under `docs/`), decision ranges, pins; `-- --write` generates `.cursor/BUGBOT.md` from `AGENTS.md`; no `AGENTS.override.md` anywhere and no `AGENTS.md` or `.cursor/BUGBOT.md` but the root two, and the size limits (D15); the snapshot detector and the count rule (D17).
