### 6.1 As built on 2026-11-22: steps 1, 2, 5, 5a, 7, 8, 9 and 10

A record of the branch `refactor/poteto-pyramid-guardrails` on 22 November 2026, so a snapshot by the D17 convention: it is corrected by a dated line, not rewritten. One commit per step, a follow-up to step 1 and three commits of review fixes; nothing is merged or pushed. Each step was reviewed on its own, and the whole branch then through six lenses. Step 1 and the step 1 follow-up did not run the integration suite at their own commit, and step 5 ran it in its first review round but not after its last change, which touched only the check script and its unit test (the commit messages of steps 1 and 5 say so, and the follow-up's build report); the full runs at steps 7 to 10 covered the tree up to step 10 (build reports, not re-run for this record). The three review-fix commits did not run it either: they changed the check scripts, `eslint.config.js`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and their unit tests, or comments and documents, and their commit messages say so. No GitHub Actions run has exercised any of it (section 7, "Not verified"). After the last review fix, on 22 November 2026, every suite was run once on the branch's final tree and passed: lint, typecheck, the unit, component and integration tests, the build, the audit, the secret scan, `check:docs`, `check:migrations` in branch and in main mode, and the browser suite (`npm run test:e2e`), whose set-up migrates and seeds through `connectMigrator`. Corrected later on 22 November 2026: the branch was then pushed and opened as one pull request, and its first GitHub Actions runs (45003041488 on the push, 45003247557 on the pull request) passed the quality, integration and secrets jobs.

**Step 1, `npm run check:docs`.**

- `scripts/check-docs.ts`, with the rules in `scripts/lib/docs-check.ts` and unit tests beside them; one step in the quality job. `-- --write` regenerates `.cursor/BUGBOT.md`.
- The count rule judges each sentence, not the cell or paragraph (D17 as amended); table cells are judged apart, so a date in another cell of the row does not count.
- The path rule also tries any `.md` path under `docs/` and beside the document; a token with no extension counts as a path only when it contains a `/` and either ends in `/`, starts with `./` or `../`, or starts with a repository folder.
- The pin rule reads workflows (job containers and service images included), compose files, Dockerfiles and any tracked script or source file that runs docker, and skips an image a compose service builds here (review fix CHK-6). A listed document that is missing is a finding, not a silent skip (CHK-5).
- `git check-ignore` refuses a whole batch when one path lies beyond a symbolic link, as in a worktree that links `infra/.generated` or `data/jobs` from the main checkout for the browser suite. The command sorts such paths out first (`splitAtLinks` in `scripts/lib/docs-check.ts`) and passes one when it is really there; found when `check:docs` stopped during that run.
- `AGENTS.md:7` carries the D15 sentence, and `AGENTS.md:36` names 0003, 0004, 0010, 0012, 0013 and 0014. One sentence on the D17 convention went into the "how to read" of `docs/security/control-matrix.md`, `threat-model.md` and `operations-and-release-checklist.md`.

**Step 1 follow-up, pin rule and secret scan.**

- `npm run scan:secrets` had reported a pass from a git worktree after reading no history. It now mounts the git common directory, and fails when gitleaks read no commit, git failed in the container, the clone is shallow, or gitleaks did not run; since review fix CHK-10 it also fails when the working-tree scan read no bytes, gitleaks logged no byte count, or gitleaks logged an error. It says how many commits it scanned (`scripts/lib/secret-scan-check.ts`, `scripts/scan-secrets.mjs`).
- The script runs under `node --experimental-strip-types`, so the CI secrets job relies on type stripping before step 13. `engines.node` rises to `>=22.18.0` in step 13 (D16), after which the flag can go.
- gitleaks is pinned by tag and digest in `scripts/scan-secrets.mjs`.

**Step 2, test hygiene.**

- `NO_SKIPPED_TESTS`, `NO_RANDOM_IN_TESTS` and the mock ban in `eslint.config.js`. The skip selector also covers `suite`, the chained forms and the options form; the mock ban covers `doMock`, `importMock` and `stubGlobal` in integration and e2e files, and its message for `packages/db/test` names that package's own helpers (LINT-3).
- The skip message says fix or delete: a declared skip has no allow-list (sections 3.4 and 4.1).
- `scripts/lib/test-inventory.test.ts` (every test file in exactly one suite, by Vitest's own matcher) and `scripts/lib/playwright-configs.test.ts` (OTH-05, over every Playwright config git lists).
- The two `Math.random()` calls became a fixed scrambled order and fixed secrets.

**Step 5, `npm run check:migrations`.**

- `scripts/check-migrations.ts`, with the rules and the git readers in `scripts/lib/migrations-check.ts`. The quality job checks out with `fetch-depth: 0`, and `.gitattributes` keeps `db/migrations/*.sql` byte for byte.
- Main mode lists no commit outside `MAIN_HISTORY_EXCEPTIONS` (empty); its message for a commit that is not a merge explains the fast-forward and rebase case (CHK-1, D12).
- `LOCKS_HEADER_FROM = 15`; the `audit_event` marker is the line `-- audit_event change: <why>` above the statement; `GRANDFATHERED` names 0001 (the audit grants) and 0002 (`sec.*` DDL outside the switch), each for its own rule only.
- Rule 4 goes past the list of section 3.3 (see there), and reads a double-quoted name as PostgreSQL folds it (CHK-9).
- `db/upgrade-tests.json` has an entry per file, sorted; an entry may give a test and a reason together, and a `test` must name its migration's file or number (CHK-4).
- `validateMigrationNames` is shared by the runner and the check. `AGENTS.md` section 4 lost only the duplicate and skipped-number clause; the other clauses stay until step 47 (section 1.5).

**Step 5a, `connectMigrator`.**

- `connectMigrator(connectionString, { applicationName })` in `packages/db/src/migrate.ts` opens a one-connection pool, checks `current_user` and `session_user` once, and returns a branded `MigratorPool`. Provisioning's six commands take that type, and `assertMigrator` is gone.
- The runner holds one client for the whole run; the seed runs each organisation in one `withOrg` transaction and imports no `pg`.
- Refusal tests: `packages/db/test/migrate.integration.test.ts` (the runtime role, the superuser, the superuser acting as the migrator and the migrator acting as `tinnox_definer`, the last two through `-c role=`) and `tests/integration/seed.test.ts`, which runs the seed as a child process against its own database.
- The membership check is a case in `packages/db/test/rls.integration.test.ts`; it filters on the member side only (SEC-6, left to step 6).
- `NO_MIGRATOR_POOL_CAST` (review fix SEC-1) refuses a cast to `MigratorPool` in every file, tests included, except `packages/db/src/migrate.ts`.
- `AGENTS.md:16` names the four connectors, the incident scripts, and the integration test helpers that connect as the migrator with plain clients to arrange data.

**Step 7, import boundaries.**

- `IMPORT_LAYERS` and `importBoundaryBlocks()` in `eslint.config.js` generate every `no-restricted-imports` block; `scripts/lib/import-boundaries-lint.test.ts` fails when a workspace has no row.
- Rule (c) gained the sibling-path alternative; rule (f) uses the browser files minus tests and `vite.config.ts`, with `NODE_ONLY_MODULES`; rule (g) also bans `fastify-plugin` and `@fastify/*` outside the API, tests included.
- `import()` and `require()` are held by a unit test over every tracked code file (`scripts/lib/load-specifiers.ts`, review fix LINT-1); a computed specifier is left to review.
- Product code may not import `scripts/lib` by relative path (LINT-12).
- The 18 route exports moved verbatim (section 3.1, rule (i)). `apps/api/package.json` exports `./test-support`, and `tests/package.json` declares `@tinnox/api`.
- `types: []` in `packages/contracts/tsconfig.json` and `packages/domain/tsconfig.json`.

**Step 8, strictness.**

- `switch-exhaustiveness-check` with `considerDefaultExhaustiveForUnions: false`; the three switches now name their cases.
- `NO_NON_NULL` (a `TSNonNullExpression` selector) in every non-test syntax list.
- `NO_INLINE_CAST` over `INLINE_CAST_FILES`, which includes `apps/player-web/sw/**`; 13 casts went.
- One `errorField` in `packages/db/src/error-field.ts`, exported from `@tinnox/db`. The other sites use `in` narrowing or members the declared types already have, and `scripts/audit.ts` uses a new `parseExceptions` in `scripts/lib/audit-check.ts`.

**Step 9, manifests and pins.**

- `scripts/lib/workspace-deps.ts` and `scripts/lib/exact-pin.ts`, tests beside them; the root package is checked as the unit that owns `scripts/` and the config files.
- Use is decided from the code: imports, npm-script commands, `@types` and peers. A load by name counts only through `LOADED_BY_NAME` (`apps/api pino-pretty`), and a deliberate pin only through `ALLOWED_UNUSED` (`tests axe-core`), both in `scripts/lib/workspace-deps.test.ts`.
- Each workspace's kind (node, browser, development) is one record, `UNIT_KIND`, which must name every workspace (CHK-7); in a browser workspace a bare built-in name is read as the npm polyfill.
- A type-only import in a runtime file counts as a runtime import, so step 17 must add `@tinnox/contracts` to `packages/db` `dependencies`.
- The lockfile was refreshed with npm 10.9.2 `--package-lock-only`; its 32 `"peer": true` flags predate the branch and will drop out with the first npm 11 write.

**Step 10, bans.**

- Web storage as a property ban in every browser file, tests included (review fix LINT-8), and the HTML and style sinks: `insertAdjacentHTML`, `createContextualFragment`, `cssText`, and from LINT-7 more parsers and `Object.assign`.
- Status wording (P2-04, DEC-33, SCR-17) over JSX text and the props that are shown or read out (the `content`, `error` and `meta` props, direct JSX children and nested conditionals from review fix LINT-2).
- Outbound: the deny-list of D23 over `OUTBOUND_SOURCES`, with load selectors; review fix LINT-4 added destructured globals, `createRequire(...)(...)` and `getBuiltinModule`.
- `process.env` outside `ENV_READERS`, `globalThis.process.env` and `import { env } from 'node:process'` included (LINT-5); the clock in `apps/api/src` outside `main.ts` and `alerts-once.ts`, with `MediaPipelineOptions.now` now required.
- `NO_CREDENTIAL_NAMES` with no override yet (step 12), `MIGRATOR_URL_READERS`, `NO_BARE_KEYBOARD` with one named exception block for `apps/portal/src/testFrame.test-support.ts`, and `no-warning-comments` (at the start of a comment only since review fix LINT-10).
- `NO_ORG_FROM_REQUEST` and `packages/contracts/src/org-id.test.ts`, which allows one schema, `PairingPollResponseSchema` (the API sends it to the player); `email` joined the logger's redacted fields, with a test over every redaction path.

**Left open by the review of 2026-11-22.** Confirmed findings that were deliberately not fixed, in whole or in part, and who should take each:

- SEC-3: `connectMigrator` checks one connection once; a replacement after a drop is not checked again. The comment says so; a guard on the pool's `remove` event is optional (no step).
- SEC-4: the `connectMigrator` error text and the `NO_MIGRATOR_POOL_CAST` message say the connection "logs in" as `tinnox_migrator`, and the comment at `scripts/provision-member.ts:71` says it refuses "any other login". The finding held that a superuser session started with `session_authorization=tinnox_migrator` would pass; the doc comment of `connectMigrator` (`packages/db/src/migrate.ts`) records that PostgreSQL 17 keeps `session_user` as the login there (checked 2026-11-22), so such a session is refused and the wording holds. The reviewer's optional `system_user` check and a `session_authorization` refusal case were not taken (no step).
- SEC-5 (known residual): `connectMigrator`, `MigratorPool`, `runMigrations` and `resetTestDatabase` ship from the root `@tinnox/db` entry the API imports (step 15).
- SEC-6 (known residual): the membership case filters on the member side only, so a grant to `tinnox_definer` passes (step 6).
- BEH-1: provisioning's commands rely on the brand alone; `NO_MIGRATOR_POOL_CAST` stops a plain cast, and a cast to an alias of the type or `as never` is left to review (no step).
- LINT-4 and LINT-5 (remainders): a `require` made by `createRequire` under another name, and `.env` on a default import of `node:process`, are not seen; the comments in `eslint.config.js` say so (left to review).
- LINT-6: the organisation-id selector misses a destructured handler parameter and an API-local schema built with `.extend` or `.merge`, and would flag `job.params.orgId` (step 18).
- LINT-9: the browser storage and `cssText` bans also flag reads, and apply in browser tests; a reasoned disable is the escape (no step).
- LINT-13: the inline-cast ban does not see `as Record<string, unknown>` or a cast to a local named type; a `Record` ban needs `apps/api/src/routes/audit.ts:91` moved to a type guard in the same change (no step).
- LINT-15: nothing checks that the paths named in lint messages exist; a unit case, or `eslint.config.js` in `check:docs`, would (no step).
- LINT-16 (known residual): runtime skips and an aliased test runner (step 3).
- LINT-17: the real-service mock ban misses `vi.spyOn(globalThis, ...)`, an assignment to `globalThis` and an aliased `vi`; none is in use (no step).
- LINT-18: the outbound ban also flags imports that open nothing, such as `STATUS_CODES` from `node:http` and `rootCertificates` from `node:tls`; split them out with `allowImportNames` when one is needed (no step).
- CHK-2 (remainder): a replaced trigger function body, statements inside `DO` blocks or function bodies, and policies on `audit_event` pass the text rule (step 6).
- CHK-8: the count rule misses `Routes: 62` and a number under a counting table header, and the header of `scripts/lib/docs-check.ts` does not state that limit (the document steps, 45 to 48).
- CHK-9 (remainder): the `CONCURRENTLY` rule also fires on `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY`, which PostgreSQL allows in a transaction; it fails closed, and narrowing it needs a rule per statement, not per line (no step).
- CHK-11: the inventory builds its Playwright suite from `tests/playwright.config.ts` only; a config with another `testDir` needs its own suite (step 4).
- PLAN-7 and PLAN-8 are settled by the rulings of 2026-11-22: the `AGENTS.md` section 4 clauses stay until step 47 (section 1.5), and the steps went up as one pull request, by Michael's decision of 22 November 2026 (section 7).

## 7. Decisions

**Status.** Michael delegated the 26 open points on 22 November 2026: "make decision based on best practice and note them down based on Poteto principles". Each point was decided from the code by one pass and then challenged by a second, independent pass that re-opened every cited file. The challenge amended 24 of the 26 and left two as proposed (D18, D19). The register entry is DEC-38 (`docs/decisions-2026-11.md`). Any row can still be amended; the check named under "Held by" is what would change with it. On 22 November 2026, while steps 1, 2, 5, 5a, 7, 8, 9 and 10 were built (section 6.1), rows D4, D5, D6, D12, D17 and D23 were amended under the same delegation; each now says what was decided and built.

**How to read a row.** The pyramid asks where a rule lives, so each decision is written in three parts: the decision, the highest layer that holds it with the step that lands the check, and what is left for prose. Prose is only ever a judgment no check can make. Where an earlier section of this plan and a row here disagree, the row wins.

**The order of priorities used.** (1) Never weaken tenant isolation, the three-role model, audit integrity or a decision the client made. (2) Prefer the option a check can hold over one a reviewer must remember. (3) Prefer the smallest change that removes the whole class of mistake. (4) Where safe, specified code and a prose rule disagree, fix the prose; where the code is unsafe, fix the code. (5) An accepted residual is written down with its reason and the check that would notice it growing.

| # | Decision | Held by (layer, step) | Left for prose |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Keep the heartbeat's 403 and protocol v1: `docs/player-protocol-v1.md` section 10 tells a player what to do on it, and a 404 there would merge two states. Layer 2, step 28: a 403 is built only in `apps/api/src/auth/`. The lint alone cannot stop a route calling such a constructor after a lookup; the step 23 test holds that. | Is a new 403 decided before any lookup? (`AGENTS.md`, judgment calls) |
| D2 | Accept two named exceptions, both refusals where nothing changed. First, `auditStandalone(pool, event)` for sign-in and device-credential refusals: a closed union of `auth.login` (denied or failure) and `player.auth_rejected` (denied), with actor id, object type and object id typed `null` and no success variant. Second, the scoped refusal-only transaction of SCR-32 (permission denied, CSRF rejected, upload refusals). The login-state wrappers take `Autocommit`. The take, the provider call and the denial do not become one transaction. | Layer 1, step 14: `auditScoped` and `auditUnscoped` take a branded `Tx`; `@ts-expect-error` cases refuse a pool and a success event. The type stops a success on the autocommit handle only; an audit written in a second transaction is closed by the audit-debt guard (step 16) and `changeOrg` (step 19). | Does a new kind of refusal belong in the union? (doc comment on the type; the residual cell of control B11) |
| D3 | Audit three things. (1) Every successful change to tenant or security state. (2) Every attempt, whatever its result, at the five actions control B11 names: sign-in, pairing, revocation, upload, publishing. (3) Authentication and authorisation refusals (CSRF, role, device credential, pairing code), thresholded as they are today. Write nothing for other business-rule refusals (in-use 409s, edit 400s, 404s), heartbeats, purges, session activity, login state, or counters under their threshold. Delete `auth.session_expired` and `security.cross_org_denied`, which nothing writes. The outcome of T17.3 stands. | Layers 2 and 3, step 17: an audit expectation per route (the actions, or `none` with a closed reason), and an audit-failure case for every action through a `Record<AuditAction, …>`, so an action nothing writes fails until it is deleted. Step 16: the guard's exempt list (heartbeat log, login state, the purges, session `last_seen_at`, the pairing wrong-attempt counter). | Is a new kind of event security-relevant? |

(the allow-list's header) |
| D8 | State the window; do not add a lock. Revocation applies to every request whose credential or signed-URL lookup starts after the revocation commits. A request already past the lookup finishes its one transaction (no overall limit: up to 5 s for a connection, then 15 s per statement), and a media response already started may run to the request timeout. One side effect is closed: the heartbeat's write takes effect only while its registration is still active, so a late heartbeat from a replaced player cannot hide the new player's report. | D12 | Git is the authority; there is no checksum manifest. On a branch or pull request, every migration at the merge-base with `origin/main` is byte-identical in `HEAD`, and added files number above main's highest. On main, the first-parent history of `HEAD` may not delete, modify, rename or retype a migration; only commits listed in `MAIN_HISTORY_EXCEPTIONS` (none today) are allowed. The check does not depend on branch protection or on branches being up to date. It has one precondition (review finding CHK-1): main-mode reads the first-parent history, so `main` must receive merge commits or squash merges only. A fast-forward push or a rebase merge puts a branch's own commits on the first-parent history, and a review fix to the branch's new migration then reads as a change to a merged one. The check's message for a non-merge commit explains this; the remedy is an entry in `MAIN_HISTORY_EXCEPTIONS` (in `scripts/lib/migrations-check.ts`) with its reason, never restoring bytes that were not on `main`. | Layer 2, step 5: `npm run check:migrations` in the quality job, rules in `scripts/lib/migrations-check.ts` with unit tests over fixture trees and histories; the checkout needs `fetch-depth: 0`. Main mode allows only the commits of `MAIN_HISTORY_EXCEPTIONS`, an empty map today. `db/upgrade-tests.json` is sorted by file name, so two branches that add the same number conflict at merge. Backstop: the runner's duplicate check. | Until a ruleset on `main` exists the check reports but does not block, and a force push defeats the history rule; that `main` takes merge commits or squash merges only is also a repository setting, part of the same ruleset (see "Still needs Michael") |
| D13 | A level counts as released when main has stood at it: 0004 and every later file in `db/migrations`, derived from the directory, with no hand-kept list and no tags. A branch's own new file is a level too, so its seeder lands with it. The migrate-forward test runs one chain: replay to 0004 and seed, apply and seed each level in turn, upgrade to `HEAD`, assert. | Layer 3, step 34: `migrate-forward.integration.test.ts` derives the levels and fails when a level has no seeder (a no-op seeder states its reason). Layer 2, step 34: in `packages/db/test/levels/**`, imports of `@tinnox/*` and relative imports that leave the folder are banned, so a seeder stays raw SQL valid at its level. | nothing |

The evidence cells of `docs/security/control-matrix.md` are long and carry dates for other claims, so a cell-level rule would have passed the stale counts step 1 exists to catch. **Steps these decisions add or change.** Step 5a (new): `connectMigrator`, the seed on `withOrg`, the role-membership test and the reworded migrator rule (D4, D25). Step 6a (new): the alert-address cut-off (D22). Step 41c (new): the version check on the three whole-document PUTs (D24). Step 7 also moves the 18 shared route exports (D6). Step 12 gains two pull requests: the allow-listed configuration input (D5) and the reviewed mail hosts (D23). Step 32 gains the conditional heartbeat write and its two tests (D8). Step 47 no longer moves the Supabase mapping (D15). The reworded 403 rule lands with the test that pins it, in step 23 (D1, D20). Since the rulings of 2026-11-22: step 12 also lands the `apps/api/src/config.ts` override of step 10's credential-name ban with its refusal (D5) and adds `REVIEWED_SMTP_HOSTS` to the reason of the `mail.ts` outbound override (D23); step 15 moves `connectMigrator` and `MigratorPool` to `@tinnox/db/migrate` (D4); step 26's SQLSTATE lint covers `apps/api/src/tags.ts` (D6).

**Still needs Michael.** Three things a decision here cannot do:

1. **A ruleset on `main`** (D12, D14): no force push, no deletion, the three `ci.yml` jobs required, not strict, with the admin bypass that status pushes need. It is a repository security setting, so it is his to create. Until it exists, `check:migrations` reports but does not block a merge. The ruleset should also turn off rebase merging and require pull requests, so that `main` receives merge commits or squash merges only (D12).
2. **DEC-13 and alert e-mails** (D23): whether "all customer data and media stay in Australia" allows an alert e-mail to a mailbox outside Australia, which DEC-37 makes possible. This is TINNOX's question.
3. **The status card for LYX-01 to LYX-05** (D18) is a proposal in Backlog until he agrees it.

How the built steps go up is settled: on 22 November 2026 Michael chose one pull request for steps 1, 2, 5, 5a, 7, 8, 9 and 10, with the commits kept one per step.

**Not verified.** The decisions were made by reading. These parts of them still need a run: that the e2e job in CI connects as `tinnox_migrator`, so the `session_user` assertion breaks nothing there (D4; the integration job passed in CI on 22 November 2026, and the browser suite, with its set-up, passed locally the same day); the lock behaviour of the cut-off predicate under READ COMMITTED (D22); that the conditional heartbeat write does not refuse a legitimate report (D8); that every CI run resolves Node 22.18 or newer, which strips types by default (D16; the run recorded in `docs/security/control-matrix.md` B12 used v22.23.4); how a matrix job is named as a required check, and whether a required check honours `if:` and path filters (D14); how PostgreSQL's `[:space:]` compares with JavaScript's `\s`, and that no stored row violates the tighter checks (D7); the run time of the migrate-forward chain (D13). The steps built on 2026-11-22 first ran in GitHub Actions on 22 November 2026, on the push of the branch and on its pull request (runs 45003041488 and 45003247557): the quality job (lint, `check:docs`, `check:migrations` in branch mode, the unit tests that load the ESLint config), the integration job and the secret scan passed in both. Still to run there: `check:migrations` in main mode, which first runs on the push to `main` after the merge, and the e2e job, which is dispatched by hand.

**Not verified in the plan itself.** Nothing in this plan was run against a database, a browser or CI. In particular: whether the proposed catalogue tests pass today; whether LYX-05 fails when spec 12 runs alone (the full runs in rounds 6 and 8 passed it); Playwright's and Vitest's JSON report shapes; that `CONCURRENTLY` and `VACUUM` fail inside the runner's transaction; that the wizard, service-worker and staging traces in section 1.7 misbehave at run time; that the text-key dialogs drop rows; that the media window year can be stored; the revocation window; whether read handlers work in a read-only transaction; whether the extra row lock proposed for `sec.session_revoke_membership` is free of deadlocks. Settled since by step 9: whether unused manifest entries were deliberate (none was, except the `axe-core` pin). Each section was checked against the code twice, by different passes; the tables for rounds 5 to 8 in section 1.7 were written by one pass each.

## 8. Search patterns behind the counts

Run from the repository root at `002c3d6`; "non-test" excludes `*.test.ts`.

- Transaction helpers: `rg -n '\b(withOrg|withoutOrg|poolAsTx|withResolvedOrg|withOrgDecision)\(' apps/api/src apps/media-worker/src` (56 lines).
